<meta>This page continues the author's introductory or defensive remarks regarding his writings, addressing critics and then appealing to those who recognize the truth of his message of universal love.</meta>

(144)

# To the Despisers.

Although it causes no loss to my health or condition if someone holds what I have written in contempt or disparages it; nevertheless, to everyone who finds this not to their liking (as it is impossible to satisfy all <term>complexions</term> <note>In the 17th century, "complexion" referred to a person's physical and mental temperament or personality type</note> at once and be pleasing to all), it is my friendly admonition that he should not display his <term>partisan head</term> <note>original: *partheyischen Kopf*; meaning a narrow-minded or biased attitude</note> against it. Instead, he should at least show <term>humanity</term> <note>original: *humanitæt*; here meaning basic civility and common courtesy</note> and not hinder others who look upon it more gladly than he does, so that it may reach their hands without his opposition—which, to my knowledge, does him no harm, unless it be that he enjoys some easily-severed <term>private profit</term> <note>likely referring to professional or financial gain from the religious status quo</note> and fears that it might be hindered by this work.

But to the others, to whom this is not only pleasing but who also feel a co-witnessing in their conscience that it is the truth: I beg you, through the dear God and Lord of us all, who provides for all people in the whole world with equal love, that they would strive and meditate with all the powers of their understanding and ability upon the means and ways by which love among men—one toward another, and also of men toward God—might be more greatly kindled. For at this present time, love is almost entirely extinguished and has transformed into an inhuman cruelty of one brother against another. In my estimation, the priests and teachers of all religions and <term>sects</term>, none excepted, bear no small blame for this, because they keep the hearts of their listeners always <term>preoccupied</term> <note>original: *præoccupirt*; filled or biased in advance</note> and prejudiced with hatred and loathing against others who are not of their <term>confession</term> <note>religious denomination or specific set of beliefs</note>. Were it not for this, there would often be better and friendlier blood among men. 

Indeed, nature teaches almost all nations this lesson, as they allow the produce of their land, their wares, and manufactures to go to other nations regardless of what religion they are; and likewise, they gladly accept and use the gifts of God that come to them from other lands. Yes, many quarrelsome teachers themselves drink the wine and beverages of the very kingdom whose inhabitants they consider damned because of "wrong" religion (as they see it), nor do they despise the minted coin of a people for their own necessities, though they recognize those same people as <term>heretics</term> <note>original: *Ketzer*; those holding "false" religious opinions</note>. Nevertheless, they are often so fierce and embittered against those same fellow humans who seem heretical to them—whose land, produce, wine, money, cloth, etc., they themselves use—that they would not be unwilling to see them completely rooted out. This, however, is entirely against God; and those who wish to be born of Him must also be full of Divine <term>mercy</term> <note>original: *Barmherzigkeit*</note> toward friends and enemies alike. Let the lovers of truth strive to help spread such things.

<summary>The author rebukes religious leaders for inciting sectarian hatred while hypocritically enjoying the trade goods of those they condemn, calling instead for a universal love modeled on nature and God’s mercy.</summary>
<keywords>tolerance, sectarianism, religious critique, universal love, trade, hypocrisy, mercy</keywords>

# THE MINERAL STONE
<note>original Latin: *LAPIS MINERALIS*. In alchemy, this refers to a stage of the "Philosopher's Stone" or a specific medicinal substance derived from the mineral kingdom.</note>

->Or<-

## The Highest Medicine
<note>original: *Artzney*. In the 17th century, "medicine" often referred to "physic"—a substance used to restore health or transmute base states into pure ones.</note>

->From<-

### The Metals and Minerals,

->Especially from <term>VITRIOL</term>.<-
<note>original: *VITRIOLO*. Vitriol refers to a group of sulfate minerals (like copper or iron sulfate). Alchemists considered vitriol a primary "first matter" for creating powerful remedies and the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

# The Sound of the Trumpet.
<note>original Latin: *Clangor Buccinæ*. This is a famous title in alchemical literature, signifying a "trumpet blast" to awaken the reader to the secrets of the Great Work.</note>

*Extract the quicksilver <note>original Latin: Argentum vivum</note>, or the Philosopher’s Stone, as much from bodies as from the quicksilver itself, because they are of one and the same nature; and you shall have the Mercury and Sulphur from that matter above the earth, from which gold and silver were generated within the earth.*

---

<margin>from</margin> the bodies as from the Quicksilver, because they are of one and the same
<margin>nature</margin>; so you will have the <term>Mercury ☿</term> and <term>Sulphur 🜍</term>
matter upon the earth, from which <term>Gold ☉</term> and <term>Silver ☽</term> are
born in the earth.
<note>The alchemist is explaining that the "Mercury" and "Sulphur" used in the laboratory are the same essential principles that naturally produce precious metals deep underground.</note>

# Preface.

It has long been world-renowned from the mineral works of <term>Isaac Hollandus</term> <note>Isaac Hollandus and his son John Isaac were 15th-century Dutch alchemists whose writings were highly influential in the 1600s.</note>, <term>Samuel Norton</term> <note>Samuel Norton (1548–1621) was an English alchemist known for his "Key of Alchemy," which sought to simplify the works of George Ripley.</note>, and others, how the ancient philosophers consistently held that they must seek and obtain their so-called Metallic or Mineral Stone from no other thing than the metals themselves and their root. 

Initially, they indeed sought this in quicksilver <note>original Latin: *Argento Vivo*</note> or common mercury <note>original Latin: *Mercurio Vulgi*</note> alone—though with much loss of time. Subsequently, they sought it in the perfect metals, especially Gold, as the most highly perfect, by means of the Spirit <note>original Latin: *mediante Spiritu*</note> or Mercurial Water <note>original Latin: *Aqua Mercuriali*</note>. Following this, they also found the Star of the Sun <note>original Latin: *Astrum Solis*</note>—which is the Soul of Gold or the Red Tincture—no less in the imperfect red metals, after first reducing them to the First Matter <note>original Latin: *previa eorundem reductione ad Materiam primam*</note>. This is possible because the Metallic Root <note>original Latin: *Radix Metallica*</note> is contained within each and every one of them in greater or lesser degrees <note>original Latin: *secundum magis & minus*</note>; they found it especially in <term>Mars</term> <note>The alchemical name for Iron.</note> and <term>Venus</term> <note>The alchemical name for Copper.</note>.

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

<summary>This preface outlines the historical consensus among alchemists that the Philosopher's Stone must be derived from metals. It explains that while earlier efforts focused on mercury or gold, the "Soul of Gold" can also be extracted from iron (Mars) and copper (Venus) once they are reduced to their primary state.</summary>

<keywords>Isaac Hollandus, Samuel Norton, Philosopher's Stone, First Matter, Quicksilver, Gold, Star of the Sun, Tincture, Iron, Copper, metallic root</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the preface from the signature mark )( 2. The previous page discussed the ancient philosophers' search for the "Soul of Gold" within both perfect and imperfect metals. This page shifts focus to the "modern" alchemists who look toward unsmelted ores and those who work with common mercury or specific "wet" and "dry" paths involving Gold.</meta>

# Preface.

The moderns have progressed from dead **metals** to their unsmelted ores and minerals; they now believe they can produce the aforementioned **Stone** <note>original Latin: *Lapidem*, referring to the Philosopher's Stone.</note> from these much better and more easily than from all the previously mentioned perfect or imperfect **metals**. However, this is to be noted with a grain of Philosophical Salt <note>original Latin: *cum grano Salis Philosophici*. This is a play on the common idiom "with a grain of salt," referencing "Salt" as one of the three primary alchemical principles (Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury).</note>.

How to proceed primarily with common **Mercury** <note>represented by the symbol ☿</note> alone is taught by <term>Johannes de Rupescissa</term> <note>A 14th-century Franciscan friar whose work *On the Consideration of the Quintessence* was foundational to alchemical medicine.</note> in *On the Preparation of the True Philosopher's Stone*, <term>Samuel Norton</term> in his *Mercury Revived*, and others, whom you may consult.

In working with Gold, as far as the preliminary work is concerned, two paths—the Wet and the Dry—have been used until now. In the former, <term>Friar Basilius Valentinus</term> <note>A legendary 15th-century alchemist, supposedly a Benedictine monk, though his works were likely published by Johann Thölde in the early 1600s.</note> and his followers worked very expensively and laboriously; they (1.) first of all purified the Gold by means of **antimony** and common sulfur, and subsequently prepared the Solar Water or "King’s Bath" <note>original: *Solarische Wasser* or *Königsbad*; known in chemistry as *Aqua Regia*, a mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids capable of dissolving gold.</note> from **Saltpeter**, **Sal Ammoniac**, and flint pebbles for the **dissolution** of the Gold into crystals.

<summary>This page describes the transition in alchemical thought from working with processed metals to raw ores and outlines the specific "Wet Path" used by Basilius Valentinus to dissolve gold using Aqua Regia.</summary>
<keywords>ores, minerals, common mercury, John of Rupescissa, Samuel Norton, Basilius Valentinus, Gold, Wet Path, Aqua Regia, Saltpeter, Sal Ammoniac</keywords>

This refers to the primal substance from which all metals were thought to originate.</note>—must be prepared to obtain the **Mercury of the Sun** <note>The "Mercury of the Sun" refers to a refined, spiritualized form of Gold.</note>. <meta>This page continues the preface from the previous page, moving from a discussion of specific chemical materials (like vitriol and mercury) to the confusion caused by "sophistic" alchemists. # Preface.

<margin>)( 4</margin>

The various operations presented here have, up to this day, caused much incorrectness, error, cost, harm, and ridicule among the **crowd of pseudo-chemists** <term>Turba Chimiastrorum; a derogatory term used by serious alchemists for those they considered charlatans or 'puffers' who followed false recipes without understanding the underlying principles.</term> and **sophistic process-mongers**. This is to such an extent that whoever cannot provide themselves with a faithful guide or an **Ariadne's thread** <note>In Greek mythology, Ariadne's thread allowed Theseus to navigate the Labyrinth; alchemists used this metaphor for the logic or secret key that leads a seeker through the confusing metaphors of alchemical literature.</note> here, must very easily fall from one erroneous path into another. This is just as **Johannes Pauperum** <note>Known as "John the Poor," an author to whom various medieval alchemical compendiums were attributed.</note> writes in his *Abridgment of the Secret of Secrets* <note>original Latin: *Abbreviatione de Secretis secretorum*; a reference to a popular medieval treatise on statecraft and occult sciences often attributed to Aristotle.</note>:

> *Many paths have been*
> *discovered and established by the great Philosophers,*
> *and many are the measures and ways which they*
> *themselves devised according to greater and lesser degrees,*
> *just as they themselves possessed a more or*
> *less clear intellect, one more so than*
> *another. And therefore among the Philosophers there are*
> *various opinions and various measures of*
> *mineral things and bodies; and for that reason*
> *one travels by one path and another by*
> *another, and because of this the minds of men*
> *remain clouded and obscured, so that*
> *they cannot discern the true from the false, etc.*

But so that I, for my own small part, might also [assist] the lovers of the **chemical sciences** <note>original: *Chimischen Wissenschafften*</note> / in one or [another...]

<meta>catchword: ande-</meta>

<summary>This page addresses the confusion and financial ruin caused by "charlatan" alchemists and quotes Johannes Pauperum to explain how the varying intellects of past masters created a labyrinth of contradictory methods.</summary>
<keywords>Turba Chimiastrorum, sophistry, Ariadne's thread, Johannes Pauperum, Secret of Secrets, mineral bodies, chemical science</keywords>

to be helpful in other ways in this matter, I did not wish to fail—after having already contributed my utmost toward the **Animal Stone** <note>original: *ad Lapidem Animalem*. Alchemists categorized their work based on the source material; the "Animal Stone" involved substances derived from living creatures, such as hair, blood, or urine.</note> three months ago—to now further produce in print some select treatises and **excerpts** <note>original: *Excerpta*</note> of the most proven **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophorum*. In alchemical literature, "philosophers" almost always refers to practitioners of the hermetic arts or "Philosophers by Fire."</note> as a **guidance** <note>original: *Hand-leitung*</note> toward the **Mineral Stone** <note>original: *ad Mineralem*. The Mineral Stone refers to the Alchemical Work performed using metals and minerals rather than organic matter.</note>. 

Indeed, as far as the **Work of Mercury** <note>original: *Opus Mercurii*. A central alchemical process focusing on the purification and "animation" of mercury to create the universal solvent or the Stone itself.</note> is concerned, I could have also included—or even placed at the very front—**John of Rupescissa’s** <note>Johannes de Rupescissa (c. 1310–1366) was a French Franciscan friar and influential alchemist who pioneered the use of "quintessence" (distilled alcohol) in medicine.</note> ***Little Book on the Preparation of the True Stone*** <note>original: *Libellum de Confect. Veri Lap.*</note>; but because it is already found in its entirety in the Latin language in the ***Chemical Theater*** <note>original: *Theatro Chim.* Referring to the *Theatrum Chemicum*, the largest and most famous collection of alchemical texts published in the 17th century.</note> *Vol. 3, page 191 & following*, likewise on *page 297 & following*, and was also published in German by **Theophilus Neander** in the ***Alchemical Heptad*** <note>original: *Heptade Alchimica*. A "heptad" is a collection of seven; Neander published a famous collection of seven alchemical tracts in 1621.</note> at Halle in the year 1621, I preferred to refer the reader there rather than enlarge this little handbook with it. 

The same applies to **Samuel Norton’s** <note>An English alchemist (1548–1621) who wrote extensively on the "revival" of chemical principles.</note> ***Mercury Revived*** <note>original: *Mercurio Redivivo*</note>, ***Vitriolated Venus*** <note>original: *Venere Vitriolata*. "Venus" is the alchemical name for copper; "vitriolated" refers to its transformation into a sulfate.</note>, ***Saturated Saturn*** <note>original: *Saturno Saturato*. "Saturn" is the alchemical name for lead.</note>, and the fourth book of **Bernard, Count of Trevisan** <note>A famous, possibly legendary, 15th-century alchemist whose writings emphasized the slow, natural growth of the Stone.</note>. For the rest, the **wet way** <note>original: *nasse Weg*. Also known as the *Via Humida*, this method involves dissolving substances in liquids or acids and using gentle heat (like a water bath), as opposed to the "dry way" involving high heat and crucibles.</note>, insofar as this little treatise...

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the author's list of authorities to a direct quotation from Marcello Palingenio Stellato. The previous page mentioned Palingenio as a source for the "Dry Way" of alchemy; here, the text provides the specific poetic verses from the Capricorn section of his work "Zodiacus Vitae," which lament the poverty of scholars and seek divine aid for the alchemical Great Work.</meta>

### Marcello Palingenio in the Capricorn section of the Zodiac of Life. <note>Marcello Palingenio Stellato was the pen name of Pier Angelo Manzolli, a 16th-century Italian physician whose "Zodiacus Vitae" was a widely read poem combining science, philosophy, and occultism.</note>

*Therefore the ancient Sages <note>original: "Sophi veteres"</note> through subtle investigation*
*Devised a certain stone, and prepared for themselves faithful provisions*
*That would remain in all places and never perish,*
*By whose aid they could see various lands and diverse kingdoms,*
*And learn very many things from many people.*
*Once, therefore, they implored the somber powers of the gods,*
*Having first poured libations according to custom:*
*Praying especially to Mercury, the Sun, and the Moon,*
*They poured forth such voices from a pure heart:*
*O glory of the world, O Titan <note>original: "Titan." In classical poetry, Titan refers to the Sun god, Apollo.</note> most beautiful of gods,*
*O Latonia <note>original: "Latonia." A name for Diana or the Moon, referring to her mother Latona.</note> who scatters the nightly shadows,*
*O unstable and fleeting offspring of Jove and Maia, <note>Mercury (Hermes), the messenger god associated with the metal quicksilver.</note>*
*To whom it is given to transform yourself into various figures,*
*Be present and peaceful, and hear our complaints.*
*Behold, we few, whose mind is sublime and genius is high,*
*To whose heart wisdom is constantly dear:*
*While we strive to see the causes of things and the secrets of nature,*
*And measure the immense Olympus <note>The heavens or the realm of the gods.</note> in our minds,*
*We are pressed by poverty, and suffer many inconveniences,*
*And fail from hunger: meanwhile, the base and mindless common folk*
*Seize everything for themselves, and gather all the coins for themselves,*
*Nor does anyone pity us: if we ask for any*
*Thing, they show the middle finger <note>original: "digitum ostendunt medium." A classical gesture of extreme contempt and mockery.</note> and mock us,*
*Wherefore, forced to dig the fields, or groom the pack-animals,*
*Or carry manure from the stables, we can scarcely seek a living,*
*And we wretches are ordered to serve fools.*
*Therefore, you gods at least take pity on us,*
*(If there is any piety and care for the good among the celestials)*
*And show an easy way by which to live honestly,*

<meta>catchword: And</meta>

<summary>This page contains a Latin poetic excerpt from Marcello Palingenio's "Zodiacus Vitae," depicting the plight of wise men who seek the Philosopher's Stone to escape poverty. The poem features a prayer to Mercury, the Sun, and the Moon, asking for divine favor and an "easy way" to sustain their noble studies.</summary>
<keywords>Marcello Palingenio Stellato, Zodiacus Vitae, Philosopher's Stone, Mercury, Sun and Moon, poverty of scholars, alchemy in poetry, secrets of nature</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues the poetic quotation from Marcello Palingenio Stellato's "Zodiacus Vitae" (Zodiac of Life). The previous page described the Sages praying to the gods for relief from poverty and for the secret of the Great Work. Here, the gods respond with an allegorical recipe for the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</meta>

*And that we may be able to investigate the hiding places of truth.* <note>original: "veri latebras"</note>
*Having heard such things, the Gods arrived, having been called upon,*
*And Phoebus <note>Apollo, representing the Sun or the alchemical Gold.</note> answered first: "O venerable offspring,*
*Worthy of heaven and the favor of the gods above, you half-gods,*
*Listen and store my words deep within your souls:*
*Seize this Arcadian youth <note>original: "juvenem Arcadium." A reference to Mercury (Hermes), who was born in Arcadia. In alchemy, this represents Quicksilver.</note>, who is unfaithful and too fleeting,*
*And kill him by immersing him in Stygian waters.* <note>original: "Stygiis lymphis." Referring to the River Styx, here symbolizing a corrosive or transformative solvent.</note>
*Afterward, let the God whom the Lemnian land <note>Vulcan, the god of fire and smithing, whose cult was centered on the island of Lemnos.</note> worships receive him*
*When he is placed in the lap of Hyale <note>One of Diana's nymphs; her name means "crystal" or "glass," suggesting a clear liquid or vessel.</note>, and let him fix the youth, raised up, upon a cross:*
*Then bury him in a warm womb and dissolve him until he is rotten:*
*The spirit departing from our body shall penetrate his dripping limbs,*
*And in a wondrous order, it shall gradually call him back*
*From the black shadows, though he was dead,*
*Clothed in a golden cloak and shining with silver:*
*Finally, cast him upon the burning coals; he shall be renewed as another*
*Like the Phoenix: and whatever bodies he touches, he will leave perfected,*
*Overcoming the laws and pacts of nature;*
*He will change appearances and put poverty to flight."*
*Phoebus fell silent after these words; the winged Cyllenian <note>Another name for Mercury, referring to Mount Cyllene.</note>*
*Nodded in agreement, and Diana <note>The Moon, representing alchemical Silver.</note>, being present, approved the same.*
*Soon they all departed, flying to the vaults of heaven.*
*Then men, turning over the hidden oracles of the divine mind*
*In their wavering spirits, only after a long time,*
*Having experienced much and with no small expense,*
*Found that art, than which no art is more worthy,*
*The fashioning of the ethereal stone <note>original: "lapidem æthereum." The Philosopher's Stone.</note>: which the profane*
*Are by no means permitted to know, and the wicked commoners seek in vain.*
*He who possesses it is able to live decently wherever he wishes:*
*He fears neither the wrath of fortune nor the hands of thieves,*
*But the Gods deem only a few worthy of such a great gift.*

<summary>This page concludes Palingenio's poetic allegory, where the gods Phoebus, Mercury, and Diana reveal the process of "killing" and "resurrecting" quicksilver to create the Philosopher's Stone. The text emphasizes that this "ethereal stone" provides total security and wealth, yet remains hidden from the unworthy.</summary>

<keywords>Phoebus, Mercury, Diana, Philosopher's Stone, alchemy, transmutation, Phoenix, Zodiacus Vitae, allegory, Arcadian youth, Lemnia terra</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the allegorical poetry of Palingenius on the previous page to a practical, though highly technical, alchemical recipe from Johannes Rhenanus. While the previous page used the gods to describe the creation of the Stone, this text provides specific laboratory steps involving chemical reagents like vitriol, salt, and iron.</meta>

# Johannes Rhenanus, Book 3 of *Chimiotechnica*,
# Chapter 5, page 158.

## *The True Preparation of Philosophical*
## *Mercury.*

<margin>C. B. V. book 1, chapter 11, number 21. R. page 142, chapter 4, number 5.</margin>
Sublimate <note>Sublimation is the process of heating a solid until it turns into a vapor and then collects again as a solid on a cooler surface.</note> common Mercury in the common way; place it in an earthenware pot, pour on warm [water], and the iron plates placed therein will immediately seize upon the spirits of Vitriol <note>Sulfuric acid or metallic sulfates.</note> and Salt contained in the sublimate, and it will be living, running Mercury, freed from its bonds; which you shall afterwards sublimate and resuscitate <note>To "resuscitate" in an alchemical context means to return a solid sublimate back into a liquid, metallic state.</note> six times. Finally, for the eighth time, still with Vitriol and Salt; but on the ninth and final time, without resuscitation, you shall sublimate it with oil of Vitriol, observing the correct proportion, and you will receive a crystalline and most beautiful sublimated Mercury. If you cast the blood of the Red Lion <note>original: "sanguinem rubei Leonis." An alchemical term for a specific reagent, often understood as the "soul" or essential tincture of gold, or sometimes a preparation of antimony.</note> upon this Mercury, so that it may take its nourishment from it and be able to saturate itself—if you can separate every external corrosive from it—you will have a Stone red and transparent like a ruby, with which all the riches of this world cannot be compared. Blessed is he who marks these words of mine with his finger and reaches the goal of fixation <note>The process of making a volatile substance stable so that it remains solid and does not evaporate when heated.</note>.

<meta>catchword: Fr. BASI-</meta>

<summary>This page outlines a specific alchemical process by Johannes Rhenanus for preparing "Philosophical Mercury" through repeated sublimations and its final transformation into a ruby-like Philosopher's Stone using the "blood of the Red Lion."</summary>
<keywords>Johannes Rhenanus, Philosophical Mercury, sublimation, vitriol, Red Lion, fixation, Philosopher's Stone, Chimiotechnica</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the practical lab instructions of Johannes Rhenanus on the previous page to the theoretical and spiritual foundations of alchemy by the famous (and likely pseudonymous) author Basilius Valentinus. The text continues the theme of Mercury but shifts from its chemical preparation to its metaphysical status as the "Spirit of Mercury," the universal source of all tangible matter. The catchword "liebende" suggests the text will continue to address "Kunstliebende" or "lovers of the art" on the following page.</meta>

# I.

# Friar BASILIUS VALENTINUS <note>Basilius Valentinus was a legendary 15th-century Benedictine monk and alchemist, though his works were actually first published around 1600 by Johann Thölde.</note>

### Treatise on Natural and Supernatural Things.

->Edited by Johann Thölde, a Hessian.<-
->Leipzig, Year 1603. page 35 and following.<-

# ON THE SPIRIT OF MERCURY. <note>original: "DE SPIRITU MERCURII."</note>

### The Third Chapter.

Although I use a peculiar style 
of writing, which will seem 
strange to many, and will also 
appear strange in their heads and 
thoughts, yet there is sufficient reason for it: 
Yes, I say sufficient, so that I may remain with my 
experience, and pay little heed to other chatter, 
because the same is hidden within my science, 
and seeing always goes before hearing, 
and the foundation <term>Grund</term> receives praise 
over the baselessness <term>Ungrund</term>. <note>In alchemical philosophy, the "Grund" (ground/foundation) refers to the stable essence of reality, while the "Ungrund" refers to the primordial abyss or void from which all things emerge.</note>
Therefore I now say that all visible, 
tangible things are made from the Spirit of 
Mercury, which precedes all earthly things 
of the entire world, and all things 
are made from it, and have 
their origin solely from it; 
for in it is found 
everything that can accomplish all that the Art- 

<summary>In this introductory passage, Basilius Valentinus defends his cryptic writing style and asserts that the "Spirit of Mercury" is the fundamental, invisible substance from which all physical matter in the world is created.</summary>
<keywords>Basilius Valentinus, Spirit of Mercury, Johann Thölde, alchemical theory, origin of matter, experience vs. hearsay</keywords>

<meta>The text on this page continues the description of the "Spirit of Mercury" from the previous page. The previous page ended with "for in it is fo...", and this page begins with "und" (implied by context) "Liebende zu suchen begehret," completing the thought that this Spirit is "found to desire to seek seekers of the art." The catchword at the bottom "segne-" suggests the next page begins with "gesegnete" (blessed).</meta>

[found] desiring to seek seekers <note>original: "Liebende," literally "lovers" of the alchemical art</note>. It is the beginning for the working of metals, when it has become a spiritual being, which is pure air, and flies back and forth without wings, and is a moving wind which, after its expulsion from its dwelling, is driven by Vulcan <note>original: "Vulcanum"; the Roman god of fire, here representing alchemical heat or the furnace</note> into its Chaos <note>original: "Chaos"; the primordial, unformed state of matter</note>, where it then enters again and resolves itself into the elements. There it is drawn up and to itself by the sidereal stars in a magnetic fashion, out of the love from which it previously went out and was worked, because its like gladly desires and takes it to itself again. But where this Spirit of Mercury <note>original: "Spiritus Mercurii"</note> can be captured and made corporeal <note>literally "fleshly" or tangible</note>, it resolves itself into a body <note>original: "Corpus"</note> and becomes a clear and pure transparent water, which is the true spiritual water and the first Mercurial root of minerals and metals. It is spiritual, impalpable, and incombustible, without any mixture of earthly wateriness <note>original: "aquosität"</note>, and is the Heavenly Water <note>original: "Himmelwaasser"</note> of which so much has been written. For through this Spirit of Mercury, all metals can be broken, opened, and resolved into first matter <note>original: "primam materiam"</note> where necessary, without any corrosive. This spirit rejuvenates humans and livestock like the eagle <note>A common alchemical reference to Psalm 103:5, "your youth is renewed like the eagle’s," signifying the restorative power of the Philosopher's Stone</note>; it consumes all evil and leads to a great age, even to a long life. This Spirit of Mercury is the Master Key <note>original: "Hauptschlüssel"</note> of my other keys about which I wrote in the beginning; therefore I will call out: Come here, you bles- <note>The text cuts off here with the catchword "segne-," likely continuing as "blessed" (gesegnete) on the following page.</note>

<summary>This page describes the metaphysical properties of the Spirit of Mercury, characterizing it as a volatile "moving wind" that can be solidified into a "Heavenly Water" capable of dissolving metals and rejuvenating life.</summary>

...[corrosives]. You blessed of the LORD, let yourselves be anointed with oil, and refreshed with water, and embalm your bodies so that they do not rot, smell foul, or stink; for the heavenly water is the beginning, and the oil is the middle—which accordingly does not burn, because it has been made from a spiritual sulfur <note>original: "Schwebel"; an archaic spelling of *Schwefel*, referring to the alchemical principle of Sulfur</note>—and the salt-balsam is physical, which is united with the water through the oil. I will provide an extensive account of this again hereafter, when I write something of them and intend to speak.

And that I may further explain what kind of essence, matter <note>original: "Materia"</note>, and form the Spirit of Mercury <note>original: "Spiritus Mercurii"</note> is, I say that its essence is blessed, its matter is spiritual, and its form is earthly; yet this must be understood through something incomprehensible. These are indeed sharp words and speech, and many will think: your proposition is all the same, and these are strange effusions that provoke such wondrous thoughts. That is true; they are strange, and they will in turn require strange people who understand such speech. This is not a writing for peasants on how they should grease a wagon, nor is it a speech for those who have no knowledge of the Art <note>original: "Kunst"; here referring specifically to the "Great Art" of alchemy</note>, even if they were ever so learned as they imagine themselves to be. But I consider him alone to be learned who, next to God's Word, establishes earthly things—which must be judged by reason <note>original: "vernunfft"</note>—through true knowledge, and

What is required, then, for the beginning of this Spirit <note>original: "Gestes," an archaic spelling of *Geistes*</note> of Mercury <term>Mercury: in alchemy, one of the three primary principles (Tria Prima), representing the spirit or volatile essence of a substance</term>? It is unnecessary for you to know this, because such knowledge can bring you no help nor profit; only understand this: that its beginning is supernatural, granted from the heavenly, the sidereal <note>referring to the influence of the stars</note>, and the elemental realms since the start of the first creation, so that it might further enter into an earthly being. But because this is necessary—as has been made known to you—leave the heavenly things to the soul and grasp them through faith; leave the sidereal alone as well, because such sidereal impressions <note>original: "Impressiones"; the invisible influences or "stamps" left by celestial bodies</note> are invisible and incomprehensible. The elements <note>original: "Elementa"</note> have already fully brought the Spirit <note>original: "Spiritum"</note> into the world through nourishment <note>original: "nutrimentum"</note>; therefore, do not trouble yourself with them, since man can make no elements, but the Creator alone. Stay instead with your prepared Spirit, which is already formal and informal, comprehensible and incomprehensible, and yet is visibly manifested before you. Thus you have enough of the prime matter <note>original: "primam materiam"; the base starting material for the Alchemical Great Work</note> from which all metals and minerals <note>original: "Minera"</note> have grown. And it is a single thing and such a matter <note>original: "Materia"</note> which unites with the sulfur <note>original: "Schwebel," an archaic spelling of *Schwefel*</note> of the following chapter, and enters into a coagulation <note>the process of a liquid becoming solid</note> with the salt of the fifth chapter, so that a body is formed from it, and a perfect medicine <note>original: "Artzeney und Medicin"</note> for all metals—not only to give birth [to them] at their beginning in the earth:

the Spirit of Mercury <note>original: "Spiritus Mercurii"; in alchemy, this refers to a universal life force or animating principle, not just the liquid metal</note> is directed and characterized from above down / upon the Animals <note>original: "Animalia"</note>, so an Animal being comes from it / if, by its bestowal, it goes upon the Vegetables <note>original: "Vegetabilia"</note>, then a Vegetable work comes from it / but if, because of its infused Nature, it falls to the Minerals, then Minerals and Metals <note>original: "Metallen"</note> come from it / yet each has its own distinction in how they are worked / the Animals for themselves / the Vegetables in another way and form for themselves / and the Minerals for themselves / each in a particular way / of which to write specifically <note>original: "in specie"</note> would be very extensive / and would require a large and diverse narrative.

<meta>This page continues the practical instruction promised on the previous page. Having established that the Spirit of Mercury is the animating principle of minerals, the author now provides a specific laboratory procedure to extract and refine this spirit into a transformative oil or essence.</meta>

and the best gold ore <note>original: "Goldertz"</note> that you can get / grind it together into equal weight / before it has been touched by any fire / pour upon it an **Oil of Mercury** <note>original: "Oleum Mercurii"</note>, made *by itself* <note>original: "per se"</note> / from common purified and sublimated **quicksilver** <note>original: "Quecksilber"</note> / set it aside / and digest it for a month / then you have an *Extraction* <note>original: "Extractio"</note>, which is more heavenly than earthly / gently distill the *Extraction* in the **Bath of the Blessed Virgin Mary** <term>Bath of the Blessed Virgin Mary: Also known as a "bain-marie," this is a vessel of hot water used to heat another container gently and evenly to prevent scorching.</term> / so that the **Phlegm** <note>original: "Flegma"; in alchemy, the watery, tasteless, and inactive distillate</note> passes over / and the heavy Oil remains at the bottom / which takes all metals into itself *in a moment* / to this pour three times as much **Spirit of wine** <note>original: "Spiritum vini"; ## II.
# *Anonymous* on the Philosophical Water.
### Printed in Kassel by Jacob Gentsch. In the year 1649.

So that I may compose and explain to you / most beloved son / in the briefest way and as if for a **memorial** <note>original: "memorial"; a summary or memorandum intended to aid the memory</note> / an understanding of the right, true **Philosophical Stone** / and how one should proceed in its **preparation** <note>original: "præparation"</note> / know then that such a stone [is made] of two things / namely **from body and spirit** <note>original: "ex corpore & spiritu"</note> / or from male and female seed / that is / from the **Water of Mercury** <note>original: "Wasser Mercurii"</note> and the cor- <note>The text cuts off here, likely continuing with "corporeal" or "body" on the next page.</note>

) 14. (

...I say to you, my son, unless the **body of the Sun** <note>original: "corpus Solis"; alchemical gold</note> be sown, otherwise it is in vain and brings no fruit, just as Christ speaks: Unless a grain of wheat falls into the earth and dies and decays, otherwise it brings no fruit. So then, when the **body of the Sun** is born anew through the Water and Spirit, there grows forth a glorified, astral, eternal, immortal body, which brings much fruit and can **multiply** <note>original: "multipliciren"</note> itself just like **vegetable life** <note>original: "Vegetabilia"</note>.

For the body is **perfect** and united with all elements and agreeable. But if it is not born anew, then nothing new, nothing greater, nothing more **clarified**, nothing higher or better can come of it.

This original essence is then a **sulfurous water** and not **living mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius vivus*; common quicksilver or liquid metal</note>, as the **Sophists** <note>In alchemy, "Sophists" were practitioners who lacked true understanding and followed literal, often incorrect, recipes</note> believe. with which he dissolves the **bodies** <note>original: *Corpora*; the solid metallic states</note>
and makes them spiritual. Therefore, note with the
highest diligence that without this water, nothing in
**chemistry** <note>original: *Chemia*</note> can be accomplished; also, without it,
everything is false and a lie, as much in metals
and minerals as in **vegetables and animals**.
One may dissolve, sublime, distill, calcine,
extract, mix, and compose with whatever one wishes;
one may dissolve it in the cellar, in the **water-bath** <note>original: *Balneo*</note>, in manure <note>used for gentle, steady heat</note>,
in **strong waters** <note>original: *Aquaforten*</note> and all kinds of powerful waters, as
it may always seem possible, and as the alchemical
processes teach, or can be so devised;
one may make oil, water, lime, powder,
black, white, yellow, and red; one may burn
it or cast it, however it is possible, and as the
alchemical recipes teach and present as truth
for the making of gold and silver—yet it is all,
in its foundation and outcome,
false, foul, and a lie. For I myself

...I have experienced all this with my own hands—because otherwise I would not have believed it—to my own harm and disadvantage. Therefore be warned, my son, to guard yourself against the boastfulness of such **sophists** <term>Sophisten</term> <note>In alchemical literature, "sophists" or "puffers" were fraudulent practitioners who used deceptive tricks to mimic gold-making, rather than following the "true" philosophical path.</note> in **alchemy** <term>Alchymey</term> and all such deceitful, loose people, just as you would against the wretched Devil, and as if against an impure, cruel, burning fire and poison;

For through this sophistry of sweetly-creeping false alchemy, a person ruins their **body and soul** <note>original: *Leib und Seel*</note>, honor and property, and this deceit is in truth worse than the Devil himself. Therefore open your eyes; recognize, recognize the single **key** <note>referring to the "water" or solvent mentioned on page 35</note> and flee all falsehood, for it is impossible to attain anything true and useful in any other way than what I have already reported in this **little book** <note>original: *Büchlein*</note>.

### III.

Anonymous (anagram: *The wave of the Tagus is in our power* <note>original: *Penes Nos Unda Tagi*, a famous Latin anagram for the author's name, Jean d'Espagnet (c. 1564–1637)</note>) in the *Secret Work of Hermetic Philosophy*. *Published in Geneva, 1673. Canons 35 to 53 inclusive.*

35. In the Philosophical Work, more labor and time are expended than expense. For to the one who possesses the proper **matter** <note>The raw material or "First Matter" required to begin the alchemical process.</note>, only small expenses remain to be sustained; therefore, those who greedily hunt for a great abundance of money, and place the difficult goal of the work in high costs, trust more in foreign riches than in their own art. Let the overly-trusting **beginner** <term>tyro</term> <note>A Latin term for a novice or apprentice.</note> beware of those plunderers; for while they promise mountains of gold, they lie in wait for Gold itself; they demand the Sun as a herald <note>original: *Anteambulonem Solem*; meaning they want to be paid in gold (the Sun) before they have even started the work.</note> because they walk in darkness.

36. Just as those navigating between **Scylla and Charybdis** <note>A classical metaphor for being caught between two equally dangerous choices; Scylla was a six-headed monster and Charybdis a deadly whirlpool.</note> are endangered on both sides, no less peril is faced by those who, gasping for the prize of the **Golden Fleece** <note>The object of the quest of Jason and the Argonauts, here used as a metaphor for the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, are carried between the treacherous rocks of the philosophers' **Sulfur** and **Mercury**. The more discerning seekers have gained knowledge of **Sulfur** through the diligent reading of serious and trustworthy authors, and by the radiance of the Sun; but they get stuck at the threshold of the **Philosophers' Mercury**. For writers have diverted it with so many circles and labyrinths <note>original: *mæandris*</note>, and shrouded it in so many ambiguous names, that it occurs more quickly to the searching mind by a sudden impulse than it is found through reason or sweat.

<meta>This page continues the excerpt from Jean d'Espagnet’s "Secret Work of Hermetic Philosophy," moving from practical warnings about financial ruin to the core theoretical difficulty of the "Great Work": defining the true nature of "Mercury." 37. So that the Philosophers might immerse their **Mercury** <term>Mercurius</term> in even deeper darkness, they have made it manifold; in every part and stage of the work, they have established a "Mercury" of their own, though each is different. No one will ever attain a perfect knowledge of this substance if they remain ignorant of any single part of the work.

38. The Philosophers have especially recognized a **triple Mercury** of their own—specifically after the completion of the first stage of preparation and **Philosophical sublimation** <note>In alchemy, sublimation is not just the transition from solid to gas, but a process of purifying a substance to its highest "spiritual" essence.</note>. At that point, they call the substance "their Mercury" and "sublimated Mercury."

39. Next, in the second preparation—which is called the "first" by many authors because they omit the actual first step—the **Sun** <note>The alchemical symbol for Gold.</note> is now "made raw" again <note>original: *reincrudato*; the process of breaking down purified gold back into its primal, "crude" state so it can be transformed.</note> and resolved into its **First Matter**. This is properly called the **Mercury of bodies**, or the **Mercury of the Philosophers**. At this stage, the matter is called **Rebis** <note>From the Latin *res bina*, meaning "a double thing"; a symbol of the union of opposites, like male and female or spirit and body.</note>, **Chaos**, or the **Whole World**, because everything necessary for the work is contained within it, and it alone suffices to perfect the **Stone**.

40. Finally, the Philosophers sometimes refer to the **perfect Elixir** and the **staining medicine** <term>medicina tingens</term> <note>A substance capable of "tingeing" or permanently changing the color and nature of a base metal into gold.</note> as "their Mercury," though they do so improperly. Strictly speaking, the name "Mercury" belongs only to a **volatile** <note>Something that easily evaporates or escapes into the air.</note> thing, because it is "sublimated" or raised up in every stage of the work. However, because the **Elixir** is entirely **fixed** <note>"Fixed" refers to a substance that is stable and can withstand the highest heat of a furnace without evaporating.</note>, it rejects the simple name of Mercury; therefore, they called it "their Mercury" to distinguish it from the volatile kind. The straight path of investigating and discerning so many "Mercuries" of the philosophers is paved only for those

> ->...those whom impartial Jupiter has loved,<-

> <note>original: "quos æquus amavit Jupiter, aut ardens evexit ad æthera virtus." A quote from Virgil’s Aeneid (Book VI), referring to the rare few capable of descending into the underworld and returning—a metaphor for the alchemical process of death and rebirth.</note>

41. The **Elixir** <term>Elixir; from the Arabic 'al-iksir', a substance believed to transform base metals into gold or grant immortality</term> is called the **Mercury of the Philosophers** because of its similarity and great conformity with the celestial planet Mercury. For just as the celestial Mercury is believed to be devoid of elemental qualities but highly prone to being influenced by them, this "versatile Proteus" <note>Proteus was a Greek sea-god capable of changing his shape at will; here he represents the adaptability of Mercury.</note> takes on and increases the nature and spirit of other planets based on its opposition, conjunction, and aspect. The ambiguous Elixir works in a similar way: because it is devoted to no quality of its own, it embraces the quality and spirit of the substance with which it is mixed, and multiplies its virtues and qualities in a wonderful manner.

42. In the **Philosophical sublimation** <note>The process of purifying a substance by turning it into vapor and then back into a solid; in alchemy, this also refers to "spiritualizing" the matter.</note> or first preparation of Mercury, a "Herculean labor" falls upon the operator; for without the help of Hercules <note>original: "Alcide", another name for Hercules.</note>, Jason would have attempted the Colchian expedition <note>The quest for the Golden Fleece in the land of Colchis.</note> in vain.

Their ferocity will be soothed only by the insignia of **Diana** <note>The Moon, associated with Silver in alchemy.</note> and the doves of **Venus** <note>The planet Venus, associated with Copper, but here representing the "gentle" or "loving" qualities needed to pacify the raw matter.</note>, if the fates call you.

Whoever calls the **Moon of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Lunam Philosophorum*; a symbolic name for the white, silver-like stage of the alchemical work</note> or their **Mercury** "common mercury" <note>The liquid quicksilver found in shops or nature</note> either knowingly deceives or is himself deceived. XLV. The experience and opinions of the more serious Philosophers support the view that this Mercury of the Philosophers is not quicksilver in its natural state, nor in its entire substance, but is rather its middle and pure essence, which has been created from it.

46. This Mercury of the Philosophers is expressed by many various names; at times it is called "earth," and at other times "water," for different reasons; and also because it is naturally forged from both. It is the **Subtle Earth**, white and sulfurous, in which the elements are fixed and "philosophical gold" is sown. It is the **Water of Life** <note>original: *Aqua vitæ*</note>, or "burning water," "permanent water," "most clear water," and "the water of gold and silver." This Mercury, because it contains its own **sulfur** <term>Sulfur; in alchemy, this represents the active, masculine, and combustible principle of a substance</term> within itself—which is multiplied by the craft—deserves to be called the Sulfur of Quicksilver. Finally, that most precious substance is the **Hermaphroditic Venus** of the ancients, possessing the power of both sexes. <note>In alchemy, a "hermaphrodite" represents the perfect union of opposites, such as Mercury (feminine/cold) and Sulfur (masculine/hot).</note>

47. Quicksilver is partly natural and partly unnatural. Its intrinsic and hidden root lies in Nature; it cannot be reached unless through a preceding cleansing and ingenious sublima- <meta>The text cuts off here, continuing to the next page with the word "tionem" to form "sublimationem" or sublimation.</meta>

...sublimation <note>original Latin: *sublimationem*</note> cannot be called forth. What is external is outside of Nature and accidental <term>accidental; in Aristotelian philosophy, an "accident" is a property that a thing has (like color or size) which is not essential to its true nature</term>; therefore, separate the pure from the impure, the substance from the accidents, and make the hidden manifest by way of Nature. Otherwise, halt your progress, for this is the foundation of the entire art and work.

48. That dry and most precious liquor constitutes the **radical moisture** <term>radical moisture; Latin *humidum radicale*. In medieval science, this was the essential, life-sustaining fluid thought to exist within all things—from humans to metals—preventing them from becoming dry and brittle</term> of Metals; it was therefore called **Glass** by some of the ancients. For Glass <note>original Latin: *Vitrum*</note> is drawn out from the radical moisture stubbornly adhering to the ashes, which yields only to the most extreme flame; yet our **central Mercury** <note>The inner, spiritual core of the matter</note> comes forth by the most gentle, though longer, fire of Nature.

49. Others have sought their **philosophical earth** through **calcination** <term>calcination; the process of heating a substance to a high temperature in the air to reduce it to a powder or "calx"</term>; others through **sublimation** <term>sublimation; the alchemical process of heating a solid until it becomes a vapor and then collects again as a purified solid</term>; some among glazing vessels; some between **vitriol** <term>vitriol; a sulfate mineral, usually green or blue, used as a powerful chemical agent</term> and salt, as if hidden within natural vessels. Others have commanded it to be sublimated from lime and glass. We, however, have learned from the Prophet <note>Moses, traditionally considered the author of the first books of the Bible</note> that:

> In the beginning God created the heaven and the earth. And the earth was without form, and void; and darkness was upon the face of the deep. And the Spirit of God moved upon the face of the waters. And God said, Let there be light: and there was light. And God saw the light, that it was good: and God divided the light from the darkness, etc. <note>A Latin translation of the opening of the Book of Genesis. Alchemists saw the creation of the world as the ultimate chemical process to be imitated.</note>

The **Blessing of Joseph** pronounced by the same prophet will be enough for the wise:

> Of the blessing of the Lord is his land; of the fruits of heaven and the dew, and the deep lying beneath; of the fruits of the increase of the Sun and Moon; from the summit of the ancient mountains; from the fruits of the hills... <note>original: *De pomis col[lium]*; This quote from Deuteronomy 33:13–15 was often used by alchemists to suggest that the materials for the Great Work are provided by "heavenly" influences like dew and the "fruits" of the celestial bodies.</note>

50. **Quicksilver** <term>Quicksilver; Latin *Argentum vivum*, another name for the element Mercury</term> is so polluted by original sin <note>The author uses theological language to describe the "imperfect" state of raw minerals found in the earth</note> that it overflows with a twofold defect. The first it contracted from the unclean earth, which mixed itself into the metal during its creation and adhered to it through freezing <note>Latin: *congelationem*; in alchemy, this refers to the solidification of a substance</note>. The other defect, bordering on **dropsy** <term>dropsy; an old medical term for swelling or edema caused by excess fluid</term>, is a fault of the "water between the skin" <note>Latin: *aquæ intercutis*; a metaphor for excess, impure moisture trapped within the metallic substance</note>. This proceeds from a thick and impure water mixed into the clear liquid; Nature could not squeeze out or separate this through compression, and because it is foreign, it flees when even a gentle heat is applied. That **leprosy** <note>Alchemists used the term "leprosy" to describe the corrosion or impurities that "sickened" base metals, preventing them from being pure gold</note> infesting the body of Mercury is not part of its true root or substance, but is accidental <term>accidental; a property that is not part of the essential nature of a substance</term>; therefore, it can be separated from it. The Earthy impurity is washed away through a moist bath and the cleansing of Nature; the Watery impurity is put to flight through a dry bath by the gentle fire of generation. Thus, through a threefold washing and purification, the **Dragon** <note>The Dragon is a common alchemical symbol for the raw, volatile matter of the work</note> is renewed, stripped of its ancient scales and filthy skin.

51. The Philosophical **sublimation** <term>sublimation; the process of heating a substance to purify it, often viewed as a spiritual "elevation" of the matter</term> of Mercury is completed by two actions: by removing the superfluous from it, and by introducing what is lacking. The superfluous things are the extreme "accidents," which cloud the glowing **Jupiter** <note>In alchemical planetary shorthand, Jupiter represents the metal tin or a specific stage of whitening and purity</note> with the dark sphere of **Saturn** <note>Saturn represents lead or the dark, "blacker than black" stage of the work known as the *nigredo*</note>. Therefore, separate the emerging blackness <note>original Latin: *livorem*; literally a leaden or bluish-black color</note> of Saturn, until the purple star of Jupiter smiles upon you. Add the **Sulfur of Nature**, of which Mercury indeed possesses enough of a grain and ferment for itself; but act so that it may also suffice for others. Therefore, multiply that Sulfur...

Not far from that fountain at the threshold, the budding violets will first meet you; these, irrigated through small streams by a vast **golden river**, will take on the most brilliant color of a dark sapphire; the **Sun** <note>In alchemy, the Sun represents Gold and the perfected masculine principle</note> will give you the signs. You shall not separate such precious flowers from their root until you compose the **Stone** <term>Philosophers' Stone; the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and acting as a universal medicine</term>; for when freshly plucked, they contain more juice and tincture <note>In this context, "tincture" refers to the concentrated "soul" or color-changing power of the substance</note>. Then, harvest them diligently with a subtle and skillful hand, for unless the fates oppose it, they will follow easily, and once one **golden** <note>original: *aureus*</note> flower is torn away, another will not be lacking. With longer care and labor, the **Lily** and the **Amaranth** will follow.

IV.

## The Paracelsian Method for extracting Mercury, Sulfur, and Crocus from all Metals.

->*From the Works of Philippus Theophrastus Paracelsus, Volume I, page 949 and following.*<-

To extract Mercury from metallic bodies is nothing other than to resolve them, or to reduce them into their "First Matter" <term>First Matter; Latin *prima materia*, the original, unformed substance from which all things are created</term>. This is to say, it is reduced to running Mercury, just as it was in the center of the Earth before the generation of metals—namely, a moist and viscous vapor, containing within itself invisibly the Mercury and Sulfur of Nature, which are the principles of all metals. Such Mercury is of unspeakable power and possesses divine secrets.

This reduction is performed by *Mercurial Water*, which was known neither to John of Rupescissa <note>original: *Johanni de Rupescissa*; a 14th-century Franciscan alchemist famous for his work on the 'quintessence'</note> nor to others, regardless of what they may boast. Therefore, it must be studied diligently and treated with tireless labor. This, then, is the way the said Mercurial Water is prepared:

Take 3 pounds of Mercury sublimated <note>Sublimation is a process where a solid is heated until it turns into a vapor and then collects again as a solid on a cooler surface, effectively purifying it</note> seven times by vitriol <term>Vitriol; a historical term for metal sulfates, used here as a powerful acidic reagent</term>, saltpeter, and alum.

Take 1.5 pounds of sal ammoniac <term>Sal ammoniac; ammonium chloride, a salt used in alchemy to help dissolve or volatilize metals</term>, which has been three times sublimated from salt and is clear and white. Ground together and reduced to a fine powder, sublimate them in a sublimatory <note>A specialized glass vessel for sublimation</note> in a sand bath for 9 hours. When it has cooled, remove the sublimate with a feather, and sublimate it again with the remainder, as before. Repeat this operation four times, until it no longer sublimates, and in the bottom...

<meta>Catchword: bottom</meta>

<summary>This page marks a shift from allegory to laboratory practice, detailing Paracelsus's specific chemical recipe for creating 'Mercurial Water,' the solvent required to break metals down into their primary components.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsus, Mercury, Sulfur, Crocus, First Matter, Mercurial Water, John of Rupescissa, sublimation, vitriol, sal ammoniac</keywords>

--- Page 60 ---
<meta>This page continues the laboratory instructions from the previous page for preparing "Mercurial Water." The process described here involves repeated saturation and distillation to produce a "Mercury of the body" from gold or silver. The catchword "Mineræ" at the bottom indicates that the discussion of metallic ores will continue on the next page.</meta>

...at the bottom a black mass should remain, flowing like wax. Once cooled, remove it and, having ground it, saturate it again in a glass dish frequently with water of sal ammoniac <term>Sal ammoniac; ammonium chloride, a salt used in alchemy as a powerful solvent and flux</term> prepared according to the art <note>original: *s. a.* or *secundum artem*, a common instructional phrase meaning "according to the rules of the craft"</note>. Saturate it and let it dry after it has coagulated of its own accord; repeat this up to 9 or 10 times, until it almost no longer coagulates. 

Grind the substance finely upon a marble slab and, in a humid place, dissolve it into a beautiful oil. You shall then rectify <note>to purify a liquid, usually through repeated distillation</note> this oil by distillation in ashes, separating it from all dregs and residue. Carefully preserve this water, which is easily the chief of all such waters. Take 8 ounces of it and place within it very fine plates of gold or silver <note>original: *Solis aut Lunæ*; the alchemical "Sun" and "Moon"</note>, thoroughly cleaned, weighing 1.5 ounces. Place this in a closed glass vessel for digestion in hot ashes for 8 hours. 

You will see your "body" <note>referring to the metallic gold or silver plates</note> at the bottom of the vessel transformed into a subtle vapor or Mercury. Once the solution is complete, separate all the Mercurial water by sublimating it through an alembic <term>Alembic; a classic distillation apparatus consisting of a boiling flask and a cooling cap to collect vapors</term> over a slow fire, drawing it away from the first matter, and preserve it carefully in a small glass vessel. In this way, you will possess the truest "Mercury of the body," the use of which in desperate circumstances—provided you use it prudently—is miraculous and heavenly; for that reason, it must not be revealed to the unworthy.

## THE SULFUR OF METALS
## BY THEOPHRASTUS.
<note>Theophrastus refers to Paracelsus, whose full name was Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim</note>

The Sulfur of Metals is an oiliness extracted from them, endowed with many virtues for the health of man. This sulfur is elicited from metals before they have undergone the fire <note>referring to metals in their raw ore state before smelting</note>, such as from golden or silver marcasites <term>Marcasite; in historical alchemy, this term was used broadly for various metallic sulfides and ores that resembled gold or silver</term>, and so on, according to the nobility of the metal.

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on metallic preparations, shifting from the "Mercurial Water" of gold and silver to the extraction of the "Sulphur"—the essential oily or soul-like principle—from various ores and minerals. The catchword "Mineræ" from the previous page begins this section.</meta>

From minerals, even noble and excellent ones: **Likewise from pyrites and cobalt, according to the nature and property of each.** <note>original: *Kiß und Koboltern*; "Kies" refers to pyritic ores or sparks, while "Kobolt" refers to cobalt ores, often feared by miners as "goblin" minerals that yielded no silver and released toxic fumes.</note>

The more common method of extraction is as follows: take the best distilled vinegar <term>Acetum; distilled wine vinegar, the primary acidic solvent of early chemistry</term> which has stood for 24 hours over the "dead head" <note>original: *caput mortuum*, the solid residue remaining after a substance has been distilled to exhaustion</note> of distilled Vitriol <note>iron or copper sulfates</note>, Saltpeter, and Alum, and has been distilled through an alembic. I say, pour this over the powdered metallic body in a glass vessel so that it stands seven fingers above it, and place it in horse dung <note>original: *fimum Equi*; a common alchemical technique where the natural heat of decomposing manure provided a constant, gentle temperature for digestion</note> to digest for nine days. Distill the colored vinegar in ashes <note>using a sand or ash bath for even heating</note> until it becomes a sulfurous oil, which you shall rectify in a water bath <note>original: *Balneo [Mariae]*</note> or in the Sun. You will then have the truest Sulphur of the metallic body itself, which you shall use rightly according to your own judgment.

Extraction can also be performed using a sharp and most purified lye <term>Lixivium; an alkaline solution leached from wood ashes</term>. However, such "sulphurs" are less suitable for internal use within the body because of the alkali of the ashes—from which we produce corrosive potash <term>Clavellatum; "potash" or potassium carbonate, used in making soap and glass</term>—and because of the lime from which such lyes are made. Sulphur extracted in this way can be washed with fresh water and precipitated. The digestion afterward requires double the time. The lye must also be rectified by its own sublimation to remove all earthy residue, so that such sulphurs do not incorporate the residue and become corrosive to the ruin of the sick. To prevent this, the aforementioned separation must be performed. So much for raw materials.

But now, extract the sulphur from those metals already melted and purified: a more certain, noble, and better way is not [given] <note>The text cuts off here, continuing with the catchword "bitur" for the Latin *dabitur*.</note>

It dissolves and breaks apart any metal, leading it out of its metallic nature into another, depending on the worker's aim and skill.

## SAFFRON OF METALS
## OR TINCTURE.

The Saffron of Metals <note>original: *Crocus Metallorum*; "Crocus" (saffron) refers to the deep orange or reddish-brown color of a metallic oxide, usually iron.</note> is of four kinds: of Gold, Copper, Iron, and Steel. <note>original: *Solis, Veneris, Martis & Chalybis*; the author uses the planetary associations for the metals: the Sun for Gold, Venus for Copper, and Mars for Iron.</note> The Saffron of Steel is superior. It is extracted by reverberation <term>Reverberatio; a method of roasting in a furnace where the flame is reflected or "reverberated" off the roof onto the material</term> or calcination, which reduces these metallic bodies to powder. In this way, iron filings are consumed by rust. *The consumption of rust occurs through the soaking of those things which create rust,* <note>original German: *Das verzehren deß Rosts geschicht durch imbibition derer ding / so den Rost machen*</note> and through boiling which draws out the color of the rust.

Take: aged urine, poured off from its sediment—several jugs of it—in which you dissolve three handfuls of ground salt. Boil the strained liquid and skim it well. In this, dissolve again a handful of crushed Vitriol <note>a metal sulfate, often iron or copper</note> and two or three ounces of crushed Sal ammoniac <term>Sal armoniacum; ammonium chloride, used frequently in alchemy to help dissolve or "open" metals</term>, and again skim it thoroughly. Soak the iron filings with this liquid and boil them until they can be turned into a powder. Then, roast this powder in a strong fire, stirring it constantly with an iron [rod]...

<meta>The catchword "ferreo" (iron) refers to the "iron rod" (ferreo bacillo) used to stir the mixture in the next stage of the process.</meta>

...[in an] iron [vessel], until it passes from one color to another, and finally into a most brilliant violet color. Through the separation of the elements, you will collect the residue <note>original German: *des abzugs im grund deß Glaſſes*; literally the "draw-off" at the bottom of the glass</note> at the bottom of the glass. With this, you can perform marvelous works both inside and outside the body.

To prepare the **Saffron of Venus** <note>original: *Crocus Veneris*; copper was associated with the planet Venus in alchemy</note>: Take 1 or 2 pounds of the best pulverized <term>alcolisatae; reduced to an extremely fine powder, a term derived from the same root as "alcohol"</term> verdigris <note>original: *Aeruginis*; the green crust or pigment that forms on copper</note>. Pour over it plenty of distilled vinegar and shake it well three times every day. Gently pour off the colored vinegar and sublimate it entirely to dryness on a bed of ashes <note>a method of indirect heating</note>. This powder should then be washed nine times with warm water to remove all sharpness <note>acidity or caustic properties</note>, and then dried. You will then have the prepared **Saffron of Venus** or **Flower of Copper**. From this, if you wish, you may easily extract an oil according to the instructions given in the *Great Surgery* <note>likely referring to Paracelsus's *Grosse Wundartzney*</note>, where its use is also explained.

**Saffron of the Sun** <note>original: *Crocus Solis*; gold was associated with the Sun</note> must be extracted through salt water, by which its metallic nature or malleability is broken. After the residue is washed with warm water, the saffron should be drawn out with spirit of wine. Once that is separated again, the saffron will remain at the bottom. By raising the heat and sublimating it five times, it is changed into a liquor, or the truest **Quintessence of the Sun** <note>the "fifth essence," the purest and most potent medicinal form of the metal</note>. With this, you will accomplish great things; for this, an experienced artist <note>original German: *erfarner Künstler*; referring to a skilled alchemist</note> is needed, and not a mere pretender.

## Friar Basil Valentine, of the Benedictine Order / Revelation of the Hidden Manipulations.
->Strasbourg Edition 1651, pages a. to q. <note>original: *Edit. Argent. 1651*; "Argentoratum" is the Latin name for Strasbourg.</note><-

### Directed towards the Universal and High Secret of the Philosopher’s Stone of Health and Riches.

In the name of the Most High God, I, Friar Basil <note>Basil Valentine is the purported author of many influential 15th-century alchemical texts, though many scholars believe these were written in the late 16th century by Johann Thölde.</note>, a cloistered servant of the Lord, begin to write the true way and manipulation, together with the entire process: how I [found] the earthly light of the highest wisdom of natural things, for the greatest joy and comfort of my health— <note>The text continues on the next page; the original German word *Gesundheit* (health) is split by the page break.</note>

truth, made it, and brought it to an end; and for the confirmation of the **Truth**, I swear by my conscience that everything I write is true, in this <term>Universal<note>In alchemical theory, a "Universal" work is one that seeks the "Great Elixir" or Philosopher's Stone capable of transforming all base metals and curing all diseases, whereas "Particular" works target specific metals or ailments.</note></term> of the necessary <term>manipulations<note>original: *Handgriffen*; refers to the practical, manual laboratory procedures or "hand-grips" required for the work.</note></term>, as shall also be mentioned at the end in the concluding remarks on the particularities at the very last part of the work. Take of the very best Hungarian gold as much as you wish, and six times as much <term>Hungarian Antimony<note>original: *Antimonij Ungarici*. Literally a 'shard,' here referring to a flat ceramic dish or shallow vessel used for refining metals.</note></term> under a <term>muffle<note>original: *Muffel*. # The True Water and Mineral Bath for the King.

## THE USE OF THE WATER. <note>original: "USUS AQUÆ"</note>

Take 3 parts of **this** water; put it into a flask, adding 1 part of the prepared <term>gold calx<note>original: "☉ Kalck"; gold that has been purified and reduced to a fine powder or oxide through heating.</note></term>. Place it in warm ashes with a <term>still-head<note>original: "Helm"; the cap of a distilling apparatus (alembic) that directs vapors into a cooling tube.</note></term>. In time, it will <term>dissolve<note>original: "solviren"</note></term>. If it has not all dissolved, pour off the water and add fresh water until all the <term>gold calx</term> has dissolved into the water. Let it cool, and it will deposit white <term>dregs<note>original: "feces"</note></term> at the <term>bottom<note>original: "fundo"</note></term>. Remove these. Pour the water together and let it <term>digest<note>original: "digeriren"; the process of exposing a substance to gentle, consistent heat to encourage chemical change.</note></term> for 8 days and nights. Remove the dregs again. <term>Digest</term> it once more for 9 days and nights <term>continuously<note>original: "continuè"</note></term>. After that, <term>distill</term> the water away so that the <term>matter<note>original: "Materia"</note></term> remains at the bottom like an oil. Take the water that was distilled off, make it warm again, and pour it back over [the oil]. Distill it off once more; pour it back over while warm. <term>Repeat<note>original: "reiterir"</note></term> this several times until the water becomes faint and weak. Finally, pour fresh water onto the oil at the bottom, <term>seal it<note>original: "verlutier"; to seal the joints of the glass vessels using "lute," a paste made of clay, lime, or other materials to prevent vapors from escaping.</note></term>, and digest it for 1 day and night. Distill <term>through sand<note>original: "per arenam"; using a sand-bath to provide a steady, indirect heat.</note></term> down to the oil. Pour the water, warmed again, back over it; distill it off frequently until all the <term>gold<note>original: "☉"</note></term> has risen over the still-head. This distillation must take place in a low flask with a flat bottom. Place the ascended <term>gold-water<note>original: "☉ Wasser"</note></term> in a cold place; in time, <term>crystals</term> will form. Separate these from the water.

<note>The symbol ☉ (Sol/Sun) is the alchemical shorthand for Gold.</note>

place these [crystals] in a small flask and pour distilled rainwater over them; in this way, the crystals will dissolve in the water. For as much <term>gold<note>original: "☉"</note></term> as was used in the work, add three times as much <term>quicksilver<note>original: "☿ vivi"; liquid mercury. In alchemy, mercury's ability to "swallow" or dissolve gold to form an amalgam was a fundamental technique.</note></term>. Swirl it around well several times, and the mercury will take up the volatile gold. Many wonderful colors will appear, and an <term>amalgam<note>A soft, putty-like mixture of mercury and another metal.</note></term> will fall to the bottom, and the water will become pure and clear. Place the amalgam on a <term>cupel<note>original: "Treibscherben"; a shallow, heat-resistant dish used to separate mercury or base metals from precious metals by heating.</note></term> and let the mercury smoke away gently while stirring, until a purple-colored powder remains, like scarlet. When this powder is put into distilled vinegar, it dissolves completely and turns blood-red, like a ruby.

### The Second Process.

**Recipe.** Take common copper; from it, make <term>verdigris<note>original: "Grünspan"; the green crust or pigment formed by exposing copper to acetic acid (vinegar).</note></term>. Grind it small and extract it with distilled vinegar. Draw it off until it reaches the consistency of an oil, then let it <term>crystallize<note>original: "schiessen"</note></term> in a cold place. In this way, you will obtain a high-grade <term>vitriol<note>original: "Vitriol"; in this context, copper sulfate crystals, valued for their purity and vibrant color.</note></term>. Dissolve this again in warm water. Let it evaporate until it is thick, then place it in a cellar so that new vitriol crystals form once more. Dissolve these again in water as before; let them crystallize again. This must be done for a third time, and thus the purification of the vitriol is accomplished. # FERMENTATION <note>original: "FERMENTATIO"</note>

## According to the Instruction of the 12th Key. <note>Refers to the "Twelve Keys," a famous series of alchemical allegories attributed to Basil Valentine.</note>

After performing the fourth work, the **fermentation** follows, which teaches how to use the well-prepared **tincture** <term>tincture: a potent, concentrated alchemical substance capable of "staining" or transforming the inner nature of base metals</term> rightly and well. Now, when your **medicine** and well-prepared **Stone** have been fully prepared from the true **Virgin’s Milk** <note>original: "Jungfrawen Milch"; a symbolic name for a white, nourishing mercurial solvent used in the process</note>, then add 1 part of this medicine to 3 parts of **Gold** <note>original: "Sol" (Sun); the standard alchemical symbol for gold</note> that has been well purified by **antimony** and beaten into very thin sheets. Melt these together in a **crucible** <note>original: "Schmelz-Tigel"</note>, first with a gentle fire for 12 hours; then let it flow continuously for 3 days and nights. In this way, the gold and the Stone are turned into pure medicine—very subtle, spiritual, and penetrating. For without the **ferment of gold** <note>original: "absque Fermento auri"</note>, the Stone cannot work or manifest its tincture, **because of its subtlety** <note>original: "propter subtilitatem"; the Stone is so refined and "spiritual" that it requires a physical metal "ferment" to anchor it so it can act upon other metals</note>. But when it is fermented and combined with its like, then the tincture has gained an entry to work upon another metal, which then **tints** <note>original: "tingirt"; chemically transforms</note> the metals in this manner.

<summary>This page describes the final preparation of the Philosopher's Stone through Fermentation, where the "Stone" is combined with purified gold to give it the physical "entry" needed to transform other metals.</summary>
<keywords>Fermentation, 12th Key, Tincture, Virgin's Milk, Gold (Sol), Antimony, Projection, Alchemy</keywords>
