## PROJECTION <note>original: "P R O I E C T I O."</note>

Take of the fermented Stone / as taught in the 1st part / 1 part to 1000 parts in the flux <note>the molten or liquid state</note> of the metals / and it will become good, stable **Gold** <note>original: "Sol"</note>. For one body <note>original: "corpus"</note> gladly embraces another body / even if it is not of its own kind / yet it must be led to it by strength and power / and be born again as its own kind; Whoever uses this means of **fermentation** / to him all stabilities are revealed / and no creature is to be compared to this subtlety / for it is All in all.

Thus the entire Work is accomplished through these IV. labors / and completed in 10. months.

This **Universal Work** <term>Universal Work: the complete process of creating the Philosopher's Stone, intended to transform base metals and provide a universal cure for illness</term> consists of 4. chapters or operations: The first part of the preparation is the **preparation** <note>original: "præparatio"</note> of the **Gold** <note>Sol</note> with its associated **solar water** <note>a specialized alchemical solvent used to dissolve gold</note> / the end of the first part is the purple-colored **Gold powder** / when the Gold has been well purged and prepared for further use.

The second part of the preliminary work is the preparation of the **Mercury** <note>☿</note> / **Sulfur** & **Salt of the Philosophers** from **Copper** <note>original: "♀" (Venus)</note> & **Iron** <note>original: "♂" (Mars)</note> / until the golden water is thickened / which is the end of the second operation; and with this the **doubled mercury** is made.

The third part of the preliminary work is the preparation of the **Sulfur of the Sun** <note>original: "Sulphuris Solis"</note>, from which to make the true **potable gold** <term>potable gold: (Latin: *aurum potabile*) a legendary medicinal liquid made from gold, highly prized by alchemists as a "panacea" or cure-all</term>; with which the third operation, and thus the most difficult labor in the Work, has been accomplished.

<summary>This page defines the final step of "Projection," where the Stone transforms 1,000 times its weight in metal into gold, and summarizes the first three stages of the ten-month alchemical process.</summary>

## The Fourth part is now the final and the After-Work / thus the last conjunction <note>original: "conjunction"; the joining together of alchemical elements</note> and uniting of all things / into one / where such things are perfected <note>original: "perficiret"</note> and brought to completion at the end.

->*The Distillation of Spirit of Wine follows.* <note>original: "Sequitur Distillatio Spiritus Vini"</note><-

A good old Rhenish wine is taken / and according to custom, a good strong distilled wine <note>brandy or high-proof spirit</note> is made from it in a distillation vessel <term>Vesica: the body or boiler of a still, named for its bladder-like shape</term>. This distilled wine is put into a glass / it must be a vial <note>original: "Phiol"</note> / first the watery parts <term>phlegmata: the watery, non-alcoholic residue or "phlegm" that comes off during distillation</term> are separated and rectified <note>purified by repeated distillation</note>, and something is always left over in the vial at the end / which one can afterwards rectify separately and use for common extraction / the test <note>original: "Proba"</note> for this is:

A little of the Water of Life <note>original: "Aqua Vitæ"; here referring to the concentrated alcohol</note> is put into a glazed ceramic shard and lit / if it burns away completely, then it is good and right / but if wateriness remains at the bottom <note>original: "aquositas in fundo"</note>, then it must be driven over <note>distilled</note> once or twice more in a tall vial / and something must be left in the bottom <note>original: "fundo"</note> of the vial / the joints of the apparatus must also always be very tightly sealed / so that the volatile spirits of wine <note>original: "Spiritus vini"</note> do not evaporate. When this Water of Life is thus distilled and well rectified / (beware that you do not come too close with a light during the distillation / lest you take harm <note>a warning about the high flammability of alcohol vapors</note>) then one puts into another vial, for one measure of this prepared Water of Life, a quarter-pound of well-sublimated tartar <note>original: "Tartari"; likely potassium carbonate or purified cream of tartar</note>, and the vial should be half-filled with it / place a spacious alembic <term>alembic: the cap or "head" of a still that redirects vapors into a condenser</term> upon it /

# VI.

## The Same Author's Concluding Discourses on the Sulphur,
## Vitriol, and Magnet of the Philosophers.
->*Ibid. pages aa. through ff. inclusive.*<-

### S E C T I O N  I.

#### Concerning the Sulphur and Ferment of the Philosophers.

I have written for you / dear seeker of the arts of secrets and the most noble blessed art of Chemistry, a small book <note>original: "Libell."</note> concerning the Philosophers' Stone / and arranged it into Twelve Keys / in which I then explicitly described the material of the Philosophers' Sulphur in the First Key; but in the Second Key, I taught <note>original: "docirt"</note> how to distill and make our water / from the Eagle and the cold Dragon <note>These are alchemical allegories: the "Eagle" usually represents a volatile spirit (like sal ammoniac or a distilled acid), while the "Dragon" represents a more fixed, earth-bound substance or mineral ore</note> / who for a long time has had his dwelling in the rocky cliffs / and slithers in and out of the caverns <note>original: "Speluncken"</note> of the earth: pour this spirit or water onto your purged and refined gold <term>☉: the alchemical symbol for the Sun, representing gold</term> let it dissolve <note>original: "ſolviren"</note> and, being well-sealed with lute <term>verlutirt: sealed with "lute," a clay or paste used to make apparatus airtight</term>, let it rot <term>putreficiren: the process of "putrefaction," a stage where matter breaks down under gentle heat</term> in dung <note>original: "in fimo"; horse dung was frequently used as a natural, steady heat source for digestion</note> for 14 days and nights / then drawn over the helm <note>Meaning distilled through the top cap of the still</note> / the water poured back onto the lime of gold <term>calcem Solis: "calx of the Sun," gold reduced to a fine powder through chemical action or heat</term> and again drawn over-

### Concerning the Vitriol of the Philosophers.
<note>original: "De Vitriolo Philosophorum"</note>

<margin><unclear>M</unclear></margin>
Dear follower of this art / I have explicitly taught you to make the Philosophers' Sulfur <note>original Latin: "Sulphur Philosophorum"</note>, which is in the place of the Male <note>original Latin: "loco Masculi"</note>, the King or Man.

Now you lack the Wife or Philosophers' Mercury <note>original Latin: "Mercurius Philosophorum"</note>, or primarily the First Matter of the Stone <note>original Latin: "Materia prima Lapidis"</note>, out of which you must make it with art. For our Azoth <note>A term for the "Universal Solvent" or the essential spirit of mercury</note> is not common vinegar / but it is extracted through common Azoth, and out of the First Matter <note>original Latin: "Materia prima"</note> a salt (which salt is called Philosophers' Mercury) is made / which then has been coagulated in the belly of the earth <note>original Latin: "Ventre terræ"</note>.

And when the starting material <note>original Latin: "materia"</note> comes to light, it is not expensive and can be found in all places; indeed, children even play with it. It is heavy and has the smell of a dead body. For about 2 florins, you can buy these materials for the work. Therefore, take the material: distill, calcine, sublime, and turn it into ashes. For any artist who has no ashes can also make no salt, and whoever has no metallic salt cannot make the Mercury of the Philosophers <term>☿ Philosophorum: the "living" essence of mercury sought by alchemists</term>.

<margin>
<image-desc>Handwritten doodle of two heart shapes and the letter L</image-desc>
</margin>

Therefore, once you have calcined the material <note>burned it to a white or grey ash using high heat</note>, extract the salt from it, rectify <note>purify through repeated distillation</note> it well, and let it "shoot" <note>a period term for crystallization</note> so that it becomes vitriol <note>a crystalline sulfate</note>. Let it be entirely sweet, without the corrosive nature or sharpness of common salt; then you have the Philosophers' Vitriol <note>original Latin: "Vitriolum Philosophorum"</note> and the Philosophers' Oil <note>original Latin: "Oleum Philosophorum"</note>. From this, proceed to make a Mercurial-water, and you will have performed a skillful work. This is then called the **Philosophers' Azoth** <note>original Latin: "Azoth Philosophorum"; a term for the ultimate solvent or "universal medicine"</note>, which **cleanses the Laton** <note>original: "Latonem," an impure metallic body or alloy, often associated with the "black" stage of the work</note> but has not yet **washed it away** <note>original: "abluirt"</note>. For the **Azoth washes the Laton** <note>original Latin: "Azoth abluirt Latonem"</note>, as the ancient Philosophers said 2,000 or 3,000 years ago. For the Philosophical Salt, or Laton, must be cleansed, dissolved, and distilled with its own moisture—its own Mercurial-water—and must attract its own magnet to itself and remain with it. This is then the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original Latin: "Mercurius Philosophorum"</note> or **Double Mercury** <note>original Latin: "Mercurius duplicatus"</note>, and these are two spirits, or a spirit and the water of the salt of metals. This water shall then be called **Juice of the Moon-plant** <note>original Latin: "Succus Lunariæ"</note>, **Heavenly Water** <note>original Latin: "Aqua Cœlestis"</note>, **Vinegar of the Philosophers** <note>original Latin: "Acetum Philosophorum"</note>, **Sulfur Water** <note>original Latin: "Aqua Sulphuris"</note>, and **Permanent Water** <note>original Latin: "Aqua permanens"</note>.

### SECTION III.
### Concerning the Magnet of the Philosophers.

Hermes <note>referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the father of alchemy</note>, a father of all philosophers, possessed the art / and first wrote thereof / and prepared the Stone from <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿</note> / <term>Gold</term> <note>symbol: ☉</note> / and <term>Silver</term> <note>symbol: ☽</note> <note>original Latin: scilicet Philosophorum; "namely, those of the Philosophers," distinguishing these substances from common street metals</note>.

This is now the <term>First Matter</term> <note>original Latin: Materia prima; the primordial, formless substance from which all metals grow</note> (otherwise previously mentioned) for the First Matter belongs to **GOD** alone / and is <term>coagulated</term> in the **bowels of the earth** <note>original Latin: Visceribus terræ</note>, and becomes first Mercury / thereafter Lead / thereafter Tin and Copper / following that Iron and so forth.

...is then a stone / from which a **volatile fire** <note>original German: flüchtiges Fewer; refers to a substance that evaporates or changes state easily when heated</note> in the form of a water is made / which drowns and dissolves its **fixed father** <note>the solid, masculine principle, often associated with sulfur or gold</note> and its **volatile mother** <note>the liquid, feminine principle, often associated with mercury or silver</note>.

<margin>Magnet</margin>
The **Salt of Metals** <note>original Latin: Sal Metallorum</note> is an imperfect body, which becomes the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original Latin: Mercurio Philosophorũ</note>, that is, a **permanent or blessed water** <note>original Latin: Aqua permanens vel benedicta; a stabilized solvent that does not evaporate easily</note> / and it is the **Magnet of the Wise** / which loves its **philosophical Mercury** <term>☿</term> <note>the specialized "living" mercury of the alchemists, distinct from common quicksilver</note>, clings to it, and remains with it. Thus our **Sun** <term>Sol</term> <note>the alchemical symbol for Gold</note> also has a Magnet / which Magnet is the first root and **Matter** <note>Materia</note> of our stone: If you understand this speech of mine / you are richer than all the world.

Hermes speaks: you must have three **species** <note>distinct types of substances</note> for the work: Firstly, **volatile water** <note>original Latin: Aquam volatilem</note> or **Mercurial-water** / **celestial water** <note>original Latin: Aquam cœlestem</note>; secondly, the

**Green Lion** <note>original Latin: Leonem viridem; often a symbol for vitriol or a raw, acidic mineral solvent</note>, that is, the **Moon of the Philosophers** <note>original Latin: Lunam Philosophorum</note>; thirdly, the **Hermetic Bronze** <note>original Latin: Æs Hermetis</note> or **Sun** or **Ferment**.

### SECTION III.
#### Concerning the Magnet of the Philosophers.

**Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the father of alchemy</note>, a father of all philosophers, possessed the Art / and was the first to write of it and prepared the Stone from **Mercury** <term>☿</term>, **Gold** <term>☉</term>, and **Silver** <term>☽</term>, namely those of the Philosophers.

<margin>y v</margin>

This is now the **First Matter** <term>Materia prima</term> (otherwise previously mentioned) for the First Matter belongs to GOD alone / and is coagulated in the **bowels of the earth** <note>original Latin: Visceribus terræ</note>, and becomes firstly quicksilver / thereafter lead / thereafter tin and copper / following that iron and so forth.

<margin>+</margin>

# ALLEGORY
## Of the Holy Trinity and the
## Philosophical Stone.

<margin>Let it be done</margin>Beloved, Christian lover of the blessed Art! How gloriously and wondrously has the Holy Trinity created the *Philosopher’s Stone* <note>original Latin: Lapidem Philosophorum</note>? For GOD the Father is a spirit, yet He allows Himself to be seen in the form of a man, as He says in His word in *Genesis chapter 1*: Let us make man, an image that is like us. Likewise, He speaks in His word and names His mouth "mouth," as well as eyes, hands, and feet. In this same way, the *Mercury of the Philosophers* <note>original Latin: Mercurius Philosophorum</note> is to be regarded as a spiritual *body* <note>original Latin: Corpus</note>, as the *Philosophers* call it.

Out of GOD the Father is born His only Son *JESUS Christ*, who is both GOD and man; He is without sin and had no necessity to die. Yet He died voluntarily and rose again for the sake of His brothers and sisters, so that they might live with Him eternally <margin>Let it be done</margin> without sin. Likewise, Gold <note>represented by the solar symbol ☉</note> is without any defect and is "fixed" <note>stable and resistant to fire</note>, so that it withstands all *tests* <note>original Latin: Examina</note> and remains glorious. But for the sake of its *imperfect* and sick brothers and sisters <note>the base metals such as lead or tin</note>, it dies and rises up gloriously, redeeming and *dyeing* <term>tingiret</term> <note>From "tincture"; the alchemical act of projecting the Stone onto base metals to transform them into gold</note> them to...

# VIII.

## Brother Basil Valentine on the Explanation of the Twelve Keys. *Strasbourg Edition, 1651, pages 166 to 168.*

I have indeed written in my previous writings about the twelve keys / from the first to the last I have directed my purpose / so that I might show my descendants in them the practice, how the great Stone of the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original Latin: Philosophorum</note> can be made from the Sulfur and Salt / of the very best purified gold / with the help of the <term>Spirit of Mercury</term> <note>original Latin: Spiritus Mercurii</note>, which must especially be hunted up and driven out from a raw, unmelted mineral ore / as indicated by way of a parable in my fifth key.

) 80 (

[diligence] must be / and is the **golden power of all metals** / raised above all power / together with the noble and white body / which is found in no thing all together / except in this / and in that which is most closely related to it. Held in very high honor by the wise, knowing, and understanding / but despised and shamed by the ignorant and blind. But whoever has had their eyes opened once / afterwards remains gladly with the truth / and becomes so eager to hide the Material <note>original: "Materiam"; referring to the physical substance used in the alchemical process</note> / and to keep it secret from the malicious / that he ponders day and night how he might hide this material from the malicious and unworthy; thus I hereby conclude my third book or part.

Reduction of a **Metal** <note>original: *Metallis*</note> into a mineral, then the minerals become Metals / so were the Metals all also **minerals** <note>original: *mineralia*</note> in the first place / therefore the **Minerals** are the **proximate matter** <note>original: *proxima materia*; the immediate material from which something is formed</note> of the Metals, and yet not the **prime matter** <note>original: *prima materia*; the original, fundamental substance of the universe</note>, from the same **Vitriol** <note>original: *Vitriolo*; metallic sulfates used as a starting material</note> further **reductions** <note>original: *reductiones*; the process of returning a substance to a simpler or more fundamental state</note> can occur: As when a Spirit is driven out from it / through the power of the Fire.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of alchemical materials, transitioning from the general theory of metallic "reduction" to the specific instructions of Eirenaeus Philalethes. (89.) X.

# Anonymous Philalethes in the Open
## Entrance. Interpreted by J. H. Cardilucius, M. D.
## in the *Great Works of Medicine and Chemistry*, page 358
### *to 361. Nuremberg Edition, 1676.*

Indeed, you could likely find our gold <note>In alchemy, "our gold" refers to "Philosophical Gold"—the living, generative essence of the metal—as opposed to the "common gold" used in currency.</note> in common gold and silver, as I have sought and found it in them myself; but this is no easy matter. Indeed, one might make the Stone <margin>all</margin> itself with easier effort than one should find the <margin>into</margin> most proximate matter of the Stone in common, commercial gold. For that reason, our gold is the most proximate <note>original: *allernächste*; the substance requiring the least amount of change to become the Stone</note>; common gold <margin><note>symbol for the Sun or Gold</note></margin> is near, but the other metals are a quite distant material of our Stone; whatever is not metallic is the furthest from it, or rather, completely estranged <note>original: *aliena*</note>.

For our gold is a chaos <note>In an alchemical context, "chaos" refers to the *Prima Materia*, the raw, unformed potential of matter before it is "fixed" into a specific form.</note>, whose soul has not been worn away by the fire. Common gold is such that its soul—so that it might be safe from the violence of the fire <note>original: *Vulcani*; referring to Vulcan, the Roman god of the forge, symbolizing the harsh heat of a furnace</note>—has <margin>firmly</margin> retreated into a locked fortress.

But if you know how to seek our gold in an intermediate thing <note>A substance that exists between the "perfect" state of gold and the "imperfect" state of base metals, often identified as antimony or "philosophical mercury."</note> between perfect and imperfect, then seek it there, and you will find it. But if you do not know it, then unlock the bolts of common gold, which is called the **preliminary work** <note>original: *Vorarbeit*; the initial purification or breaking down of the metal</note>, in which the <margin>away</margin> enclosure <note>original: *einfässelung*; the physical structure or "shell" of the metal that binds its essence</note> of its body is dissolved; otherwise <insert>ruin</insert>...

F 5
<meta>catchword: falles (meaning "in case [of]" or "falling")</meta>

<summary>This page features a passage from Philalethes explaining that while the "Philosophical Gold" can be found in common gold, it is easier to extract it from an "intermediate" substance. It describes common gold as a "locked fortress" that protects its soul from fire, requiring a "preliminary work" to unlock.</summary>

--- Page 109 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical comparison between "common gold" and "our gold" (Philosophical Gold). The previous page ended with the discussion of why common gold's soul is locked away; this page clarifies that because common gold is "fixed" and "dead," it cannot act as the active, "male" principle in the chemical marriage unless specifically treated.</meta>

...since it can by no means take the place of the male. If you now take the first of these paths, you must work with the gentlest fire; but if you take the latter, you must avail yourself of the assistance of the fiery Vulcan <note>original: *Vulcani*; the Roman god of the forge, here symbolizing high, intense furnace heat.</note>. That is, you must use such a degree of heat as we are otherwise accustomed to using in Multiplication <term>Multiplication: The stage of the Great Work where the power and quantity of the Alchemical Stone are increased.</term>, when one adds common, corporeal gold or silver as a ferment <note>A substance added to "ripen" or catalyze the transformation of the Elixir.</note> in order to bring the Elixir to complete perfection.

In this, truly, you will find a confused labyrinth or maze, unless you have the understanding to unravel yourself. Regardless of which path you choose, you require an equal, constant heat, whether you work with common gold or with our gold.

In both cases, however, you should know that with our gold, you will reach the final end two or three months sooner than with common gold; also, the Elixir will immediately be of a thousandfold potency in its first completion, whereas in the other manner, it will hardly be hundredfold.

Furthermore, when you perform the work with our gold, you must feed it, soak it (<gloss>imbibe</gloss> <note>original: *imbibere*; the process of saturating a dry substance with liquid until it can absorb no more.</note>), and ferment it, etc., through which its power will increase infinitely. In the other manner, however, you must soften it and "wax" it (<gloss>incerate</gloss> <note>original: *inceriren*; from the Latin *incerare*, meaning to bring a substance to a soft, waxy, and fixable state.</note>), as is taught abundantly in the *Rosary* <note>Referring to the *Rosarium Philosophorum* (The Rosary of the Philosophers), one of the most famous illustrated alchemical treatises of the 16th century.</note>. Furthermore, if you work with our gold, you may calcine it <term>Calcination: Reducing a metal to a fine powder through dry heat.</term>, putrefy it <term>Putrefaction: The stage of "rotting" or breaking down the matter, usually indicated by the appearance of a black color (the *nigredo*).</term>...

<summary>Philalethes explains the practical differences between using "common gold" and "philosophical gold," noting that while both work, philosophical gold is significantly faster and results in a much more potent Elixir.</summary>

XII.

## Johann Rhenanus's *Chymiotechnica*
### *Book 3, Chapter 7, in its entirety, from the Key of Theophrastus Paracelsus. Pages 159 & following. Frankfurt Edition, 1635.*

Take verdigris <note>original: *viridis æris*; a green pigment or rust that forms naturally on copper, often used as a starting material in alchemy</note> as much as you wish; pour over it distilled vinegar <note>original: *aceti destillati*; used here as a solvent to dissolve the copper acetate</note> to the height of five fingers’ breadth; digest it in a warm place, and separate the vinegar by decanting. Afterward, pour on fresh vinegar until all the greenness has been extracted. Mix these solutions together, and draw off all the moisture from them in a **water bath** <term>water bath: (*balneum Mariae*) A vessel of hot water used to heat a secondary container slowly and evenly, preventing the material from burning.</term> until no more moisture passes through the **alembic** <term>alembic: The top part of a still, used to catch and condense vapors during distillation.</term> and a crust like a skin appears on the surface of the matter. Then place it in a cold place, and you will receive vitriol <note>original: *victriolum*; a term for crystalline metal sulfates</note> of the highest quality. Take this vitriol and dissolve it in oil <note>likely "oil of vitriol," or concentrated sulfuric acid</note> to which three parts of water have been mixed; and everything that does not dissolve in a warm place within a day and a night, set aside. Repeat this work as many times as necessary until all of the said vitriol is dissolved. Then, having drawn off the moisture in a **water bath** until a crust appears, place it in a cold place so that it may congeal into little stones [crystals]; and you will receive a vitriol that is in every way the best, which in an instant coats an iron knife with a copper nature <note>This refers to a displacement reaction where copper ions in the solution form a metallic coating on the iron surface, a process often viewed by early chemists as a "transmutation" of the surface.</note>, and this is the **Vitriol of Venus** <term>Venus: The alchemical and planetary name for copper.</term> exalted to the highest degree by the **spagyric art** <term>spagyric art: A Paracelsian chemical method involving the separation of a substance into its components, their purification, and their subsequent recombination.</term>. Such vitriol is also found naturally in the bowels of the earth, though not in all places.

The most noble [vitriol] originating on the Island of Cyprus and

# Johann Rhenanus’s Chemical Technology.
## Book 3, Chapter 7, in its entirety, from the Key of Theophrastus 
## Paracelsus. page 159 and following. Frankfurt Edition, 1635.

Take as much verdigris <term>verdigris: (*viridis æris*) A green pigment or crust formed on copper, often used in alchemy as a starting material.</term> as you wish. Pour over it distilled vinegar to the height of five fingers’ breadth. Digest it in a warm place, and separate the vinegar by pouring it off <note>original: *per inclinationem*; the process of decanting a liquid to leave the sediment behind.</note>. Afterwards, pour on fresh vinegar until all the greenness has been extracted. Combine these solutions and draw off all the moisture from them in a water bath <term>water bath: (*maris balneo* or *balneum Mariae*) A vessel of hot water used to heat a container gently and steadily, named after the legendary alchemist Mary the Jewess.</term> until no more moisture passes through the alembic <term>alembic: A distillation apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube.</term> and a crust like a skin appears on the surface of the material. Then place it in a cold spot, and you will obtain vitriol <term>vitriol: A sulfate of a metal, in this case, copper sulfate, known for its glassy, crystalline appearance.</term> of the best quality. 

Take this vitriol and dissolve it in an oil <note>In this context, "oil" likely refers to a concentrated acid or oily chemical solution.</note> to which three parts of water have been mixed. Set aside everything that does not dissolve in a warm place over the course of a day and night. Repeat this labor as many times as needed until all of the said vitriol is dissolved. Then, once the moisture has been drawn off in a water bath until a crust appears, place it in a cold spot so that it may congeal into little stones <note>This refers to the formation of crystals.</note>. You will then receive a vitriol that is excellent in every way, which instantly coats an iron knife with a copper nature <note>This refers to a chemical reaction where copper precipitates out of the vitriol solution onto the iron; alchemists saw this as a sign of the "Venusian" essence within the stone.</note>. This is the **Vitriol of Venus** raised to the highest degree by the spagyric art <term>spagyric art: A term coined by Paracelsus referring to the process of separating and recombining the essential components of a substance to purify it, often translated as "to separate and to join."</term>.

Such vitriol is also found born in the bowels of the earth, though not in all places. That which originates on the Island of Cyprus is the most noble and...

<header>XII.</header>

## Johann Rhenanus’s *Chymiotechnica*
### Book 3, Chapter 7, in its entirety, based on the key of Theophrastus Paracelsus, page 159 & following. Frankfurt Edition, 1635.

Take as much as you wish of **verdigris** <term>verdigris: (*viridis aeris*) A green pigment or crust formed on copper, typically basic copper acetate, used here as the starting material.</term>. Pour over it **distilled vinegar** to a height of five fingers’ breadth; digest it in a warm place, and separate the vinegar by pouring it off. Afterward, pour on fresh vinegar until all the greenness is extracted. Mix these solutions together and draw off all the moisture in a **water bath** <term>water bath: (*maris balneo*) A vessel of hot water used to heat another vessel gently and uniformly, preventing overheating.</term> until no more moisture passes through the **alembic** <term>alembic: A chemical apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube, used for distilling volatile substances.</term> and a crust like a skin appears on the surface of the material. 

Then place it in a cold place, and you will receive **vitriol** <term>vitriol: Historically, a sulfate of a metal; in this context, copper sulfate.</term> of the finest quality. Take this vitriol and dissolve it in **oil** <note>original: *oleo*; in this context, likely "oil of vitriol" or concentrated sulfuric acid.</note> with which three parts of water have been mixed, and set aside everything that does not dissolve in a warm place over a day and a night. Repeat this labor until all the aforementioned vitriol is dissolved. 

Then, once the moisture has been drawn off in a water bath until a crust appears, place it in a cold place so that it may come together into little stones <note>original: *lapillos*; referring to the formation of crystals.</note>; and you will receive a vitriol that is in every way the best, which instantly coats an iron knife with a **copper nature** <note>A classic alchemical test for copper: dipping iron into a copper sulfate solution causes copper to precipitate onto the iron surface, making the iron look like copper.</note>. This is that **Vitriol of Venus** <term>Vitriol of Venus: High-purity copper sulfate, named after Venus, the goddess and planetary symbol associated with the metal copper.</term> exalted to the highest summit by the **spagyric art** <term>spagyric art: The alchemical process of separating, purifying, and recombining the constituents of a substance to enhance its properties.</term>. Such vitriol is also found born in the bowels of the earth, but not in all places.

In the island of **Cyprus** <note>Cyprus was famous in antiquity for its copper mines; the word "copper" (Latin *cuprum*) is derived from the island's name.</note> there originates the most noble and

[This natural vitriol] is the best, because it consists of pure, unadulterated copper without any mixed dregs or impurities, which the skillfully conducted test demonstrates more than enough. Since Cyprus is far from us, that "sapphire" <note>A reference to the striking deep-blue color of high-quality copper sulfate crystals.</note> of vitriol rarely reaches us through the merchants without being adulterated. This is the very "Vitriol of the Philosophers," which we mention in our *Treasury of Treasuries*, as well as in our *Little Book on Vitriol*. From this, through the artifice of <term>cheiropraxias</term> <note>original: *cheiropraxias*; from Greek, meaning manual practice or the work of one's own hands, distinguishing practical lab work from mere theory.</note>, a very red oil can be elicited, which is more effective against great defects than many can believe. 

Thrice and four times blessed is he who knows how to walk this path without stumbling; scarcely one in a thousand can reach this goal. For those sophists and vagabond imposters go about everything the wrong way and think nothing is right except what they do themselves. They persuade themselves that this fortified castle of nature can be stormed and captured in a moment; meanwhile, they neglect the goal toward which they ought to direct their eyes, and thus they are "entirely mistaken." <note>original: *toto errant coelo*; literally "they err by a whole sky," a common Latin idiom for being completely wrong.</note> Nature cannot be compelled by force; there is need for patience and labor; she is not captured by a single assault. For many days or years, many men, captured by love for the outstanding beauty of maidens, court them with unwearied zeal, and 

<summary>The author compares laboratory-made copper sulfate to expensive natural imports from Cyprus, arguing that their own "Vitriol of Venus" is a superior starting point for alchemical work. He critiques impatient "sophists" who expect quick results, emphasizing that nature requires long, patient labor to reveal its secrets.</summary>

[...toward which they] attack, and they do not desist from their undertaking until they have stormed them and become masters of them. Why then do we think that such a precious <term>prize</term> <note>original: *brabeum*; a Greek-derived Latin term for the prize given to winners in a race or contest.</note>, such an opulent treasure, should come to us without labor? That blessed land, which flows with milk and honey, cannot be taken without sweat and innumerable adversities, as any son of true doctrine will easily recall to his memory.

There is also another method of making this vitriol, by which it is attained with less expense; but because not all things are to be revealed or explained to everyone on account of the unworthy—for we consider it contrary to custom and practice to thrust a morsel into a crow's mouth without any prior labor—even though many could be helped by a few words, that process shall remain sealed with the "seal of art" until the age of <term>Elias the Artist</term> <note>A legendary figure in alchemical lore, first mentioned by Paracelsus, who was expected to appear at the end of time to reveal all the secrets of chemical arts and transmute the world.</note>.

### XIII.

->Marcus Friedrich Rosenberger in *Lower Astronomy* <note>original: *Astronomia Inferiori*; a common alchemical term based on the Hermetic idea "as above, so below." If "Higher Astronomy" is the study of stars, "Lower Astronomy" is the study of metals and minerals within the earth.</note>.<-

The vitriol, in the preparation of its tincture—however it may be made—must be freed from all <term>accidents</term> <note>In period philosophy, "accidents" are the external, non-essential qualities of a substance (like color or smell) that must be stripped away to reach the "essence."</note>, made entirely pure, and have all its metallic, "Venus-like" foulness <note>original: *Venerische unart*; referring to the impurities or harsh qualities associated with copper, which is the metal of the planet Venus.</note> removed from it, becoming entirely sweet and lovely. But without following the instructions of <term>Basilius</term> <note>Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were highly influential in the 1600s.</note>, you will not accomplish this in a year, and without the following manual techniques, you will not achieve your aim.

...the **Venus Tincture**, which is attached to all **vitriol**—especially the **Cyprian** variety—is mixed <term>per minima</term> <note>original: *per minima*; meaning on the most fundamental or microscopic level.</note> with aluminous, arsenical, and corrosive **salts** in such a way that it can never be removed from them through mere **dissolving** and **crystallizing** without special practical technique and knowledge. Instead, it must be taken from them, segregated, and separated quite purely according to the **Wagnerian method**. And the more you dissolve the vitriol and let it crystallize, the more harsh, sour, and hot it becomes. The reason for this is that in such a **solution**, the <term>terrestria</term> <note>original: *terrestria*; the "earthy" or coarse, impure matter.</note> and impurities are partly deposited, allowing the **corrosive salts** to mix all the better. To counter this, do as follows: in 8 days you will <term>dulcify</term> <note>original: *dulcificiren*; the process of removing the acidic, "sharp," or corrosive nature of a substance to make it "sweet" or mild.</note> 30 pounds, whereas otherwise you might accomplish nothing in a year; and the vitriol will become as sweet as an **Anima** or **Salt of Saturn** <note>original: *Sal ex Saturno*; refers to Lead Acetate, often called "Sugar of Lead" because of its sweet taste, despite being highly toxic.</note> made from lead. Then one may proceed further.

Take **native Hungarian or Cyprian Vitriol** <note>original: *Vitrioli Ungarici nativi seu Cyprij*; high-quality copper sulfate minerals.</note>—have nothing to do with any other—crush it and **dissolve** it in just enough distilled rainwater as is necessary for it to dissolve, and no more or too much water. **Filter** through paper while it is warm; place it in a flask with an **alembic** <note>The upper part of a distilling apparatus.</note> in a <term>B. M.</term> <note>original: *Balneum Mariae*; a water bath used for gentle, indirect heating.</note>. This should not boil, but be so hot that one cannot bear a hand in it, and yet not too hot. Let it stand for 2 days and nights. In such <term>putrefaction</term> <note>In alchemy, this refers to a period of slow digestion or decomposition where impurities settle.</note>, the <term>feces</term> <note>original: *fæces*; the dregs or solid sediment that settles at the bottom.</note> will **settle** two fingers thick and more. Pour off the clear liquid and let it stand again in the water bath as before, so

the <term>dregs</term> <note>original: *fæces*; the solid impurities or sediment that settle at the bottom of a liquid.</note> will settle again. Another <term>method</term> <note>original: *modus*.</note>: Take the vitriol and <term>dissolve</term> <note>original: *solvir*.</note> it as described above. Pour it into a new iron pan and let it come to a boil just once. In this way, <term>iron</term> <note>original: *Mars*; the alchemical name for iron, associated with the planet Mars.</note> will magnetically attract all the <term>copper</term> <note>original: *Venus*; the alchemical name for copper, associated with the planet Venus.</note> to itself. Thus you will have a delightful, sweet, and entirely pure vitriol which has lost all its accidental <term>salts</term> <note>original: *Salia*.</note>, and yet still contains its secret <term>mineral tinting spirit</term> <note>original: *Spiritum tingenten mineralem*; the subtle essence or "tincture" believed to be responsible for the mineral's transformative powers.</note> within it.

Or: Pour the dissolved vitriol into a copper pan, let it boil, and immediately—if there are 10 <term>pounds</term> <note>original: *lb.*</note> of vitriol—add 2 pounds of iron filings or <term>hammer scale</term> <note>The black flakes of iron oxide that fall from red-hot iron when it is forged.</note>. The vitriol will then drop all its filth and corrosive nature. Filter it and place it in a water bath to evaporate and let it crystallize; it will become as lovely and sweet as sugar. From 1 pound, barely 20 <term>loth</term> <note>A historical unit of weight; in this period, there were usually 32 loth to a pound, so a significant amount of "corrosive" weight is lost during purification.</note> will remain, and it is excellent for <term>transmutation</term> and medicine. This vitriol <term>calcines</term> <note>Turns into a dry, white powder through the loss of its crystalline water.</note> itself into a snow-white color just by sitting in a warm room.

NB. This vitriol is very easily melted; therefore, in <term>distillation</term>, a very gentle fire must be used. It also readily releases its <term>mercurial spirit</term> <note>original: *Spiritum Mercurialem*; the volatile, "living" essence of the mineral.</note>

willingly pass over; otherwise, if the <term>fire</term> <note>original: alchemical symbol Δ</note> is too strong, the matter fuses together and does not let the spirit pass. For because the instrument is low and <term>sulfur</term> and <term>salt</term> are heavy, they strike the top and immediately fall back down; however, the spirit, being the subtle part, passes over.

This spirit accomplishes much, and in this work nothing can be fundamentally achieved without it: it unlocks all metals and carries them over with it. <note>original: *Hactenus*; Latin for "thus far" or "so much for this."</note>

Whether any artist might also be served by this, I leave to his own judgment. *Test all things; hold fast to what is good.* <note>original: *Omnia probate, quod bonum est, tenete*; a quote from 1 Thessalonians 5:21.</note> If you obtain the <term>Universalist</term> <note>Likely referring to the "Universal Medicine" or the Philosopher’s Stone, which acts upon all things.</note> in this way—of which I still have my doubts—I grant it to you with all my heart.

XIV.

### Jodoci van Rehe’s Process for the Philosophical Miracle-Work from Native Vitriol, that is, from the native or purely grown vitriol, or rather according to Basil Valentine, prepared from the Vitriol of Mars <note>Iron</note> and Venus <note>Copper</note>. <note>original: *ex Vitriolo Nativo... vel potius juxta Basilium Valent. ex Vitriolo ♂. & ♀. confecto*</note>

IT is known to everyone that GOD, the Creator of all creatures, created heaven and earth out of a mass and dark chaos <note>original: *Chao*; referring to the formless state of matter before the biblical creation.</note>, that is, out of a shapeless dough and lump. He especially created man to cultivate the soil and, for the preservation of the same, He placed two fruitful spirits in the earth: a vegetative one for plants and herbs, and the other a mineral spirit to give birth to and create mines, metals, and minerals. And just as all grain, fruit, herbs, etc., grow from the vegetative spirit, so all metals and minerals come from the mineral spirit. Therefore, the whole great, wide world is called the <term>Macrocosm</term> <note>The "Great World"; the universe viewed as a complex whole.</note>, and the small world—namely man—is called the <term>Microcosm</term> <note>The "Little World"; the human being viewed as a miniature reflection of the universe.</note>, because everything that is in the great world is also in man. GOD made Adam out of a tender clod of earth and breathed breath into him, a living spirit, which was immediately so rooted in Adam that his Eve [partook] of this living spirit—

In this water, however—because I had used the <term>vitriol</term> <note>A sulfate mineral, often iron or copper sulfate.</note> in its <term>uncalcined</term> <note>original: "uncalcinirt"; not yet roasted or burned to a powder to remove moisture.</note> state—the <term>phlegm</term> <note>original: "Phlegma"; a watery, tasteless, and medically "cold" distillate regarded as an impurity in this stage of the work.</note> and watery impurity still remained.

4. Therefore, I put my menstrual water into a flask and drew off all earthly phlegm and wateriness from it in a <term>Bain-Marie</term> <note>original: "B. M." or "Balneum Mariae"; a gentle water bath used to heat substances evenly without burning them.</note>, so that not a single drop remained. And thus, to my great joy, I found at the bottom of the flask my <term>Chaos</term> <note>In alchemy, "Chaos" refers to the primordial, concentrated state of the matter before it is organized into the "Stone."</note>—beautifully dark red, thick, and very heavy in weight. This delighted me so much that I was almost entranced by it (for in my first <term>chaotic</term> work, because the matter had not been previously purified, I did not find the Chaos so beautiful, but rather mixed with many other <term>spirits</term> <note>original: "Spiritibus"; volatile vapors or essences.</note>). Because of this, I concluded with total certainty that from then on I would produce a completely pure work.

5. 114.

It is held among the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Philosophos"; in this context, refers specifically to alchemists or natural philosophers.</note> that the most high God has adorned this substance with wondrous gifts above all others. They have veiled its <term>secret</term> <note>original: "arcanum"; a hidden, powerful essence or mystery.</note> with figures and riddles, such as this: *You shall visit the interior of the earth; by rectifying, you shall find the hidden Stone, the true medicine.* <note>original: "Visitabis interiora terræ, rectificando invenies occultum Lapidem veram medicinam." This is the famous alchemical motto whose initials spell "VITRIOL," serving as a mnemonic for the process of purification.</note> By "earth," they understood <term>vitriol</term> <note>original: "vitriolum"; a term for metal sulfates, specifically green iron sulfate, which alchemists viewed as a foundational "green lion" or starting material.</note> itself, and by the "interior of the earth," they meant its sweetness and its redness. For within the secret part of subtle vitriol, a noble and most fragrant juice and a pure oil lie hidden. The method of extracting this is by no means to be attempted through <term>calcination</term> <note>The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or oxide.</note> or <term>distillation</term>. Indeed, it must not be deprived of its <term>greenness</term> <note>original: "viriditate"; often interpreted as "viriditas," the vital green life-force that alchemists sought to capture and refine.</note> in the least; for as soon as it is stripped of this, it must necessarily lack both its secret and its powers.

It should certainly be noted in this place that not only minerals, but even plants themselves and similar things that show a green color on the outside, contain within them a red oil like blood, which is their secret essence. From this, it is clear that the distillations of the <term>apothecaries</term> <note>original: "pharmacopœorum"; early chemists and pharmacists.</note> are useless, vain, and foolish, and of no importance, because they do not know how to draw out the blood-red essence of plants. Wise Nature herself turns the waters of all plants first into a <term>citrine</term> <note>A lemon-yellow color, representing a specific stage in alchemical maturation.</note> color, and thereafter into an oil as red as blood. The primary reason this happens more slowly is the excessive haste of the ignorant workers performing the distillation, by which the greenness is consumed. They have not learned to strengthen Nature with her own forces, by which that noble greenness ought to be <term>rectified</term> <note>To purify or refine a substance through repeated processing.</note> into redness by its own accord. An example is white wine, which <term>digests</term> <note>The alchemical process of slow, gentle heating, similar to incubation.</note> itself into a citrine color; and in the course of time, the green color of its grape cluster [turns] into red under blue...

<meta>catchword: lie hidden</meta>

<summary>This page explains the symbolic and chemical significance of "VITRIOL," arguing that its true power lies in a hidden red oil that must be carefully extracted without destroying its initial "greenness." The author criticizes standard apothecary methods for being too hasty and failing to respect the natural, slow maturation of substances.</summary>

<keywords>VITRIOL, alchemical riddles, greenness, red oil, distillation, apothecaries, nature's maturation, citrine stage</keywords>

--- Page 140 ---
<meta>Continuing from page 114, which cautioned that the "greenness" (viriditas) of vitriol—its vital life-force—must never be destroyed through harsh heat, as this greenness contains the hidden "secret" and power of the substance.</meta>

<page-num>115</page-num>
<sig>H 2</sig>

...turns into what is hidden within itself. Therefore, if the greenness of plants and minerals is lost through the laziness of the operators, both the essence and the spirit of the oil, and of the most noble secret balsam, will perish.

### *Special instruction on the process of Vitriol towards the red tincture.*

<term>Vitriol</term> <note>original: "Vitriolum"; a metallic sulfate, usually iron sulfate, which was a cornerstone of alchemical practice.</note> contains within itself many muddy and viscous imperfections; for which reason its greenness must be drawn out by water many times, and <term>rectified</term> <note>The process of purifying a substance through repeated distillation or filtration.</note>, until it sheds all the impurities of the earth. Once all these rectifications are finished, one must be especially careful that the material is not exposed to the sun, because this turns its greenness into paleness, and the secret essence is absorbed at the same time. It should be kept covered in a warm <term>chamber</term> <note>original: "hypocausto"; a heated room or furnace, used here as a controlled environment for alchemical incubation.</note>, so that it is not dirtied by dust. Afterward, let it be <term>digested</term> <note>A process of gentle, continuous heating used to mature a substance.</note> in a sealed glass for a period of several months, and for so long until various colors appear, and finally a supreme <term>redness</term> <note>original: "rubedo"; the final stage of the alchemical Great Work, signaling success and transformation.</note>. 

Nor should the redness be considered sufficiently fixed by that process alone, unless it is further purged from the internal and accidental impurities of the earth in this manner: It must be rectified with vinegar until the earthly pollution is entirely removed and the dregs are separated. This is the true and best rectification of its <term>tincture</term> <note>A concentrated medicinal or alchemical extract.</note>, from which the blessed oil is to be drawn. From this, once it is more carefully enclosed in glass with an <term>alembic</term> <note>A lid or apparatus used for distillation.</note> placed on top and the joints sealed with <term>bitumen</term> <note>A sealant or "lute" used to make the glass joints airtight.</note> lest the spirit escape, the spirit of that oil must be drawn out by distillation with a gentle and slow fire. This oil is more delightful and sweeter than any <term>apothecary's balsam</term> or aromatic, lacking all sharpness. There will remain at the bottom of the <term>cucurbit</term> <note>original: "cucurbitæ"; the lower, gourd-shaped vessel of the distilling apparatus.</note> a residue of earth...

<meta>catchword: which [quæ-]</meta>

<summary>This page provides specific instructions for refining vitriol into a "red tincture" and a "blessed oil," emphasizing gentle heating and the avoidance of sunlight to preserve its vital essence. It details the transition from the "green" stage of the material to a perfected "red" state through prolonged digestion and distillation.</summary>

<keywords>vitriol, red tincture, rectification, digestion, alchemical oil, purification, laboratory practice, rubedo</keywords>

--- Page 141 ---
<meta>Continuing from page 115, where the text discussed the purification of Vitriol and the emergence of the "redness" (rubedo) as a sign of progress, warning that the substance must be protected from sun and dust.</meta>

<page-num>116</page-num>

...a certain very white substance, shining and glowing like snow <note>original: "nivis instar"</note>; keep and protect this from all dust. This very earth has been entirely separated from its redness. From this follows the greatest <term>secret</term> <note>original: "arcanum"; a hidden mystery or specialized knowledge within alchemy.</note>, namely the <term>super-celestial marriage</term> <note>original: "supercœleste matrimonium"; a metaphorical union of the spiritual and physical components of a substance, often framed in religious language.</note> of the soul, which has been highly purified and washed by the blood of the lamb, with its own shining, bright, and purified body. This is the true super-celestial marriage, by which life is prolonged until the final and destined day.

In this manner, therefore, the soul and the spirit of <term>Vitriol</term> (which is its blood) are joined with its purified body, so that they may be inseparable forever. For this reason, take this our <term>foliated earth</term> <note>original: "terram nostram foliatam"; a stage of the material where it appears flaky or layered like leaves, indicating a high degree of refinement.</note> in a glass vial, and gradually pour its oil over it. The body will instantly receive and embrace its soul; for the body is greatly moved by a desire for the soul, and the soul is most perfectly delighted by the embrace of the body. Keep such a union, placed in the <term>furnace of secrets</term> <note>original: "arcanorum furnum"; a specialized alchemical oven designed to maintain a precise, steady temperature over many weeks.</note>, for forty days. 

When these days have passed, you will have a most absolute oil of wonderful perfection, by which <term>Mercury</term> and any other imperfect metals are turned into gold. Now let us apply our mind to <term>multiplication</term> <note>The final stage of the alchemical Great Work, where the power of the "medicine" or elixir is increased so that a small amount can transform a large amount of base metal.</note>. Take two parts of corporeal Mercury, and pour over it three parts by weight of the said oil, and let them remain together for forty days. By this proportion of weight and order, multiplication occurs infinitely.

->THE END.<-

This union produces a perfected oil capable of transmuting base metals into gold and increasing its own potency through infinite multiplication.</summary>
